The implementation uses compact feedback in each assembled normal input and an optional read-only `inspect_context` tool for its detailed inventory and agent-selected hypothetical policy changes. The tool names the frozen input that produced the current response: it excludes that response's subsequent tool results, rather than guessing unknown results. A hypothetical preview is not admission or a context update. Actual whole-batch admission remains authoritative. Feedback and tool descriptions count toward the actual input estimate. Save/reopen/export fixtures and both native protocols are the exit; real-model adoption and fourteen-page completion remain unverified. The existing storage/runtime and retention policies remain the baseline.

Implementation reconciliation: the inventory includes retained authored tool arguments as well as evidence results, so a large drafted Markdown argument is not invisible to the agent's cost decision. A successful edit receipt is not classified as a document read. Argument references bind to the following matching receipt within input order, since call IDs can repeat across batches. Feedback self-cost uses measured monotonic text width to avoid digit-boundary oscillation. These directly related defects were reproduced and corrected during bounded independent review. Exact reviewed identities, parent-led final checks, pressure-fixture adjustments and delivery limits are recorded in [recorded implementation result](../history/IMPLEMENTATION_HISTORY.md#stage-b-complete-nursing-input); the raw review is evidence, not a competing contract.

The resulting API is an optional saved-version index plus full/raw/pointer/range reads and frozen-input feedback. It does not adopt automatic document eviction, a mandatory preview or a compulsory model confirmation round. Both fresh precise reads and a smaller reread alongside an earlier full result are verified; only an explicit admitted release changes the latter. Earlier discussion recommendations below retain their historical status. No live conversion or additional capacity-calibration experiment is included in this implementation.

## Observations and evidence strength

| Observation | What it supports and what remains unknown |
| --- | --- |
| The user reports seeing approximately 1% quota remaining before Pause, then restored quota before Continue, and later explicitly confirms that Nexus and Codex share the OpenAI subscription quota. | The initial account-association uncertainty is resolved by the user's clarification. The offline diagnosis below finds local weekly-gauge observations consistent with exhaustion and restoration. This does not prove quota caused the later restored-quota timeout or attribute account-wide consumption entirely to conversion. |
| The initial third request failed on `tool_calls: null`; the parser was corrected at `8414c32`. | A confirmed application defect predating the user pause. Retained local checks support the correction; this is not evidence of a new unresolved parser issue. |
| Fresh page images were repeatedly summarized before a normal conversion request received their pixels; selection was corrected at `fbaddcd`. | A confirmed context-delivery defect with retained originals. It affected continuation into the remaining pages; it does not establish that the saved eight pages contain wrong text. |
| The main conversation reported two drafts/eight saved pages at 20 consumed requests. At the later pause, 60 attempts had been consumed without another saved version; the final successor left the same head at 61. | A material saved-progress observation. The 20-request checkpoint is a contemporaneous main-message report, not a new raw-ledger calculation in this discussion. The difference does not prove every intervening request was wasted: extraction, checking and organization can be useful without creating a version. |
| The main conversation subsequently read the retained request timeline: the second save follows request 19; the 20-request report is a later observation point. The 30 accepted normal replies after that save contain no further document edit/save calls. After the image fix and before Pause there are five accepted normal replies, three accepted compactions and one interrupted compaction. | These counts are attributed to the main conversation's read-only analysis of `audit.json` and the existing context observation, not a new independent calculation by the side assistant. The shorter post-fix interval must not inherit all pre-fix failure claims. Absence of another save action is different from a save operation failing. |
| After the selection fix, normal requests `:41:53` and `:44:58` contained five recent images each; `:42:55` contained the full pages 9–14 text extraction. Rereads and higher-DPI requests continued without a new draft. | The previous missing-pixel explanation alone is insufficient for this later segment. Necessary checking, loss of useful work state during summarization, tool-feedback limitations and model choices are candidate explanations, not established causes. |
| A crop-coordinate error was followed by an agent correction and successful retry. | An example of useful autonomous recovery. A tool error by itself is not proof that a new application mechanism is needed. |
| User Pause interrupted compaction `:45:59`; explicit recovery later created `:45:60`, which ended after 180.043 seconds without a complete accepted reply. | Two distinct events: user-triggered interruption and a subsequent local request timeout. Both upstream outcomes remain unknown. A local timeout identifies the stopping mechanism, not the upstream cause. |
| Bounded review found no confirmed substantive transcription error in the saved pages 1–8, and preservation/rendering checks passed. | Useful partial results remain. Pending visual/emphasis checks and incomplete scope remain explicit; preservation, successful image delivery and full content acceptance are different conclusions. |

The main commentary's statement that the successor failure was not due to the total allowance must be read narrowly: the application did not stop because of its total request/time ceiling. It does not exclude an upstream quota, queue, processing or connection cause. The maintained current-state account already preserves this uncertainty; the discussion records why the distinction matters to the user's concern.

## Discussion chronology

1. **User concern:** after seeing the main closeout recommend investigating the timeout first, the user described the approximately 1% quota observation and asked whether problems during conversion deserved the primary focus. The user requested discussion and a read of the main conversation.
2. **Assistant assessment:** the side assistant read the Stage B start, progress, interruption and recovery messages plus relevant repository status and source-review limits. It distinguished known corrected application defects, the unexplained absence of later saved progress, the user's pause and the successor timeout. It recommended a bounded analysis of the conversion progress chain rather than treating the latest error as a complete explanation.
3. **Clarification of next-step wording:** the user asked what deciding how to narrow the next step meant. The assistant clarified that the decision is which concrete question to investigate, what evidence is missing and what evidence would support a fix or continuation choice. Scope should follow the actual cause across relevant modules; no arbitrary line-count or three-example limit was proposed.
4. **Recording and handoff decision:** the user proposed updating project documents first. The assistant proposed this topic record and concise synchronization of CURRENT_STATE, PHASE_PLAN and REVIEW_HANDOFF, with facts, user observations and unadopted recommendations separated. The user approved that documentation-and-discussion handoff. The technical remedy and live continuation remain undecided.
5. **Main reconciliation and adoption:** the main conversation agreed with the progress-first focus, distinguished the request-19 save from the request-20 observation, and narrowed the post-fix sample. The user then approved that investigation direction and authorized document synchronization followed by offline diagnosis. This adoption selects the question and evidence work, not a remedy or another request.
6. **Shared-subscription clarification:** during the offline analysis, the user explicitly confirmed that Nexus and Codex use/share the same OpenAI subscription quota, and both stop when it runs out. Treat this account relationship as user-confirmed. The earlier uncertainty about the association is historical, not an unresolved user question. Newly read local gauge observations are consistent with exhaustion and later restoration; the later timeout's direct cause remains unresolved.

## Selected offline diagnosis, not implementation

Use the retained run to compare the successful eight-page save, representative pre-fix repeated read/compaction sequences, and the short post-fix interval: intended next action, evidence obtained, content actually present together in the next normal request, handoff content, subsequent agent action and saved document changes. Distinguish useful checking, obsolete state and repeated acquisition without assuming their cause. Assess document quality separately from process efficiency.

Separately determine what the existing interruption, latency and available local connector records can distinguish without reading credentials. The diagnostic outcome should identify supported causes, excluded explanations, missing evidence and a proportionate next decision. Increasing the request timeout or sending a replacement alone would not establish sustained progress. A general network/recovery redesign is not a prerequisite established by these observations.

Questions still to decide:

- Which observed handoff between evidence acquisition, input assembly, summary, agent action and saving explains the post-page-8 gap, and which part remains uncertain?
- Which retained sequences can distinguish useful verification from repeated work caused by lost information or unclear feedback? Which conclusions require a new authorized experiment?
- What observable result would justify the selected fix or continuation, while preserving existing drafts, full source scope, agent autonomy and consumption records?

There is no requirement here to diagnose every possible failure, force a specific tool sequence, force compaction, discard the saved draft, or repeat the entire project review. Any subsequently selected implementation or experiment needs its applicable authorization.

## Offline diagnosis completed 2026-10-26

### Scope, identity and method

The main conversation and two bounded read-only subagents inspected the retained native payloads, accepted replies, tool results, handoffs, compaction events and relevant production code at `49e6941`. One subagent compared input coavailability; the other examined handoff continuity. The main conversation cross-checked the material payload transitions, recalculated request/latency totals, and read allowlisted local Nexus telemetry columns. No Runtime execution, model request, new test, fault injection, source rendering, task write, credential read or code change was performed. Only this discussion and its three existing status/plan/handoff pointers were updated; no commit was created.

Evidence root `E` below means the unchanged `.local/stage-b-2026-10-26-nursing/` directory. Request ordinals are one-based; suffixes share job `d515f0cb35994683903454b325a5b3a0`. `E/chronicle/final/records.json` supplies request and operation records; `objects.json` resolves exact references to `E/preserved/final/objects/<sha256>`. `E/audit.json` supplies a convenient timeline and event index. Original raw records and hashes remain the evidence; this later diagnosis is not added retroactively to the existing all-products ZIP.

### Observed evidence-to-action chain

All normal requests in the table have no object deferrals. A summary/reference is not the complete absent source body. Image counts describe native payload parts matched to stored image bytes, not proof of model decoding, understanding or audit. Character counts exclude extraction metadata.

| #19 `:14:18` | Full pages 5–8 text and five images for pages 5–8/4 | Full five-block prior document, 17,681 Markdown characters | Saves 12 blocks/pages 1–8 as `4111f86431234edf87b68c568849fb1f`. |

### Capacity explains the alternating representations, not the whole editing decision

| Compaction | Older groups summarized | Exact group retained | Recorded normal-input estimate before → after |
| --- | --- | --- | ---: |
| #25 `:19:24` | `:15:20`–`:18:23` | None | 149,202 → 40,349 |
| #30 `:23:29` | `:19:25`–`:22:28` | None | 153,155 → 42,343 |
| #34 `:26:33` | `:23:30`–`:25:32` | None | 154,412 → 43,174 |
| #53 `:41:52` | Extraction `:38:49` and page-10 render `:39:50` | Image/document batch `:40:51` | 142,051 → 109,330 |
| #55 `:42:54` | Image/document batch `:40:51` | Newly reread extraction `:41:53` | 137,343 → 70,006 |
| #58 `:44:57` | Extraction `:41:53` and page-10 render `:42:55` | Image/document batch `:43:56` | 143,436 → 110,437 |

Events 109, 129, 146, 220, 226 and 240 in `E/audit.json` anchor these transitions. The configured input allowance is 122,880 (131,072 minus 8,192 output reserve). These are local estimation units, not provider token counts. In the first post-fix alternating step, the complete existing input plus newly read full extraction exceeds that allowance by 14,463. The older image/document group is summarized, so the next normal request contains exact text but no images. Later the converse occurs. No `context_update`, pin or release was used in this live task.

This is consistent with [retention_target](../../src/pdfmd/context.py): it prefers the newest complete result batch when a fitted handoff continuation can retain it. It does not promise to retain every earlier full representation. The fresh-image correction did reach real inputs; the later behavior is not the same never-submitted-image defect. It is an observed limitation of the present acquisition/retention interaction. It does not prove that every full body must coexist, that a smaller agent-selected view would be insufficient, or that a better planner can fit this complete payload unchanged. The successful request-19 coavailability is a useful contrast, not a controlled proof of why the model edited then.

The handoff message grows from 17,714 characters at #19 to 22,938 at #54, 23,295 at #56 and 24,044 at #59. Post-fix three accepted compactions use 417.874 recorded request seconds and 20,504 reported output tokens; five normal replies use 42.376 seconds and 487 output tokens. Across the whole task, 13 accepted compactions use 1,602.526 seconds/77,772 output tokens, versus 45 normal replies at 441.782 seconds/12,658 output tokens. These exclude unknown attempts and are not a bill or proof that every summary was wasteful. They identify a material organization burden while no additional document was offered.

The estimator counts UTF-8 bytes plus fixed/native-image allowances. For example #54 estimates 109,330 locally while its accepted provider usage reports 30,771 prompt tokens. These measurements have different meanings; the difference is not authority to raise capacity or assume a provider token limit. Calibration or a different request representation is a candidate investigation, not a selected correction.

### Handoff semantics and recoverable tool friction

The examined handoffs preserve the exact active head/parent, 12-block inventory, completed pages 1–8, unfinished pages 9–14, source references, cross-page continuation, difficult table work and instructions to save. All standalone record IDs in eight examined handoffs resolve. A wholesale loss of task identity, saved work or evidence references is not supported.

Pre-fix summaries do overstate historical non-delivery. `handoff:…:15:19` says "Only image metadata was supplied" despite actual pixels in normal `:14:18`. `handoff:…:23:29` says "Pixels were not delivered" for crop `aa342dee9cf24d7ca3aecc3e52242090`, which is present in `:22:28`. The current projection's `images=False`/`pixels_delivered:false` supplied an ambiguous basis for historical inference. The correction at `fbaddcd` adds current-projection-only guidance; post-fix handoffs explicitly distinguish this from earlier supply or understanding. Their lack of a *recorded completed audit* is not itself a false assertion. Broad inspect-all-pages cautions persist, but actionable conversion steps also remain. The successful second save occurred after an earlier misleading audit caution, so that caution is not established as the sole cause of the later stall.

Relevant responsibilities are [context.py](../../src/pdfmd/context.py): `COMPACT`, `group`, `retention_target`, `compact_request` and `apply_handoff`. History is reconstructed as a current view; compaction does not receive a separate historical native-image-delivery fact inventory. Handoff acceptance checks structure, revision, coverage and capacity, not factual truth of every visual-access claim. Normal guidance already says to save useful work early. No absence of that instruction or unavailable edit tool was found.

Repeated extraction reads retrieve the same immutable ref `7366799e8a7d4b6db953ea76d2f153f9` (26,154 bytes); they restore availability but add no extraction facts. The two post-fix `/content/blocks` reads exactly equal the existing 12-block array. Their `complete:false` denotes a field selection rather than omitted blocks; it is not evidence of hidden truncation. Higher-DPI renders provide different pixels, but normal replies record no specific newly legible label/table cell or reason, so their incremental semantic benefit cannot be measured from these records.

The three failed tools do not explain a persistent stop: the outside-page crop in `:20:26:0` is corrected in the next call; `/blocks` and an unselected document range fail in `:27:35:0`/`:28:36:0`, followed by a successful full document read. The first document error is only `'blocks'`, which is weak recovery feedback, but the agent recovered. Improving such diagnostics is a secondary opportunity, not the established cause of the final gap.

### Separate interruption and quota findings

Read-only local evidence added in this diagnosis: `%APPDATA%/Msty Nexus Runtime/nexus.db`, allowlisted scalar columns of `usage_events` and `gauge_history_points`, plus table counts. No credential, provider configuration, authorization header or request body was read from that database. The observed local listener is `msty-nexus.exe` runtime 0.8.1. Its `runtime.log` modification time precedes this run; `inference_traces` and `inference_attempts` contain no rows. No external endpoint was queried.

| Event | App evidence | Corroborating local Nexus observation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| User Pause, 2026-10-26 01:57 HKT | `:45:59` starts 01:55:49, stops after 72.225 seconds; stored reason `cancelled` | Usage row `nxs_req_L_SQcVNVFGkkkS5WKp-5hA` finishes 01:57:01, duration 72,202 ms, gateway status 502, upstream status 0, usage not recorded. |
| Authorized successor, 2026-10-26 21:02–21:05 HKT | `:45:60` ends after 180.043 seconds without accepted/partial reply; reason `unknown_outcome` | Usage row `nxs_req_F-BBm-8wzuS4T_eQFkkaow` finishes 21:05:07, duration 179,924 ms, gateway status 502, upstream status 0, usage not recorded. |
| Subscription gauge around Pause | User reported about 1% before Pause | Weekly gauge: 23% at 00:59:13, 0% at 01:59:13 HKT. |
| Gauge before successor | User reported restored quota | Same Codex subscription gauge: 89% at 20:59:13 HKT. |

The user subsequently confirmed Nexus and Codex share this OpenAI subscription quota. The observations support an exhausted shared external resource around the first stop and restoration before the successor. They do not establish the exact moment of exhaustion, attribute all account consumption to this conversion, or explain why the restored-quota successor timed out. The local gauge and gateway have different internal provider IDs; that does not override the user's account clarification and was not investigated through credentials.

Gateway rows are correlated by route, completion time and duration; adjacent successful rows also match app usage/latency. The app does not retain these Nexus correlation IDs, so this is a strongly matching local correlation rather than an end-to-end signed identity. `upstream_status=0` and token counts of zero with `usage_recorded=0` mean unreported, not a real HTTP status or proof of zero upstream work/cost. Gateway 502 at the local disconnect is not proof of a prior independent upstream 502. Successful post-fix compactions lasted 137.803, 137.251 and 142.820 seconds; the paused request was stopped sooner. The later deadline is a local stopping mechanism, not an isolated upstream diagnosis.

[nexus.py](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py) currently maps HTTP transport exceptions and the wall deadline to one unknown-outcome message; HTTP status is assigned only after the exchange completes. Existing records cannot distinguish upstream quota refusal, hidden connector retry, processing delay or another transport failure for the successor. Safe exception/correlation telemetry would be a possible later improvement; it is not implemented or a prerequisite for redesigning the whole network layer.

### Conclusion, alternatives and next decision

**Established:** pre-fix image delivery and historical-access ambiguity were real app problems; their scoped correction changes the later native inputs. Post-fix requests then alternate between large exact evidence groups under the configured estimator. Correct head/scope/refs survive, original files and drafts survive, the agent does not offer another edit/save, and text-only summaries dominate accepted request time in this short interval. Shared quota exhaustion is relevant to the user pause; the restored-quota successor's upstream cause remains unknown.

**Not established:** a new failed-save defect, corrupted drafts, vanished task scope, continued never-submitted fresh images in the corrected sample, an unavoidable model inability to convert the remaining pages, equal-quality benefit from changing the summary, or provider capacity headroom sufficient to ignore the app estimate. The agent's hidden reason for not editing is unobservable. The evidence supports a working-evidence continuity problem to investigate, not a uniquely proven post-fix code defect.

The main recommendation is to choose a bounded request-planning/continuity comparison before another unchanged full continuation. Reconstruct the recorded #54→#55→#56 and #57→#58→#59 transitions, preserving complete originals, exact chosen read bodies, native images, tool pairing, current head, scope and consumed/frozen boundaries. Determine whether removing redundant framing or using a faithful, smaller handoff can keep the required representations jointly available within the same configured limit. Do not assume a hand-written smaller summary is equivalent; identify every retained requirement/reference/caution and compare actual assembled inputs. If the complete bodies cannot fit, the next candidate is explicit capacity/current-view feedback that lets the agent choose its working evidence using existing full/field/range/pin tools, rather than automatic semantic subset selection. This is a proposed next experiment/design question, not a selected implementation or a test executed here.

Alternatives have different costs: unchanged continuation may progress because the corrected sample is short, but risks repeating the observed acquisition/compaction cycle; merely increasing the timeout addresses neither input alternation nor missing output actions; automatically forcing page batches, read counts, saves or evidence downgrades would constrain autonomy without demonstrated need. Replacing the graph/framework has no supporting evidence in this diagnosis. The existing exact originals, native request archive and durable drafts should be retained in any option.

Observable acceptance for a later selected improvement: original failing cycles are replayed with complete valid request groups; coavailability or explicit agent-selected representation decisions are inspectable; no hidden truncation, lowered scope or capacity/budget reset occurs; historical submission is distinguished from comprehension; a scripted mechanism check remains labeled scripted. Only a separately authorized real continuation can establish that the agent produces further source-faithful, saved/reopened/exported content across pages 9–14. It need not use compaction or any prescribed tool sequence. Existing eight-page review is reused within its limits, not redone or discarded by default.

This offline diagnostic increment is complete. The next remedy, local comparison and any live continuation remain for the user's decision. No implementation, new experiment or model call follows automatically.

## Bounded offline request comparison — 2026-10-27

### Question, authorization and exit recorded before execution

The user authorized one offline request-assembly and working-evidence continuity comparison, using the two retained cycles #54→#55→#56 and #57→#58→#59. ### Results and option assessment

The bounded comparison is complete. Its reusable script, exact cost/decode/hash assertions, results and explanatory report are in `.local/stage-b-request-comparison-2026-10-27/` (`compare.py`, `results.json`, `inputs.sha256.json`, `README.md`). It uses immutable final records/objects through a read-only facade and production Context/native assembly, never Store/Runtime or the live task database. Four normal payloads (#54/#56/#57/#59) reconstruct exactly, including native images and calls; the complete pre-compaction inputs reproduce 137,343 / 143,436. Forty archived input files and seven source modules were hashed and checked unchanged. No model/network request, task mutation, formal test/code change or Git checkpoint occurred.

All numbers below are local estimation units, not provider token counts. The unchanged normal-input allowance is 122,880 after reserving 8,192 for output.

| Cost in the complete input | Before #55 | Before #58 |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Existing twelve-block document result | 39,287 | 39,287 |
| Full pages 9–14 extraction result | 27,764 | 27,764 |
| Five / six configured image charges | 20,480 | 24,576 |
| Handoff message | 23,267 | 23,618 |
| System, tools, outer envelope, fixed reserve | 17,264 | 17,264 |
| Anchor | 1,109 | 1,109 |
| Calls, other results, labels and separators | 8,172 | 9,818 |
| Total | 137,343 | 143,436 |

**Lossless JSON representation:** removing only separator whitespace from JSON-bearing message strings saves 893 / 955, leaving 136,450 / 142,481. Decoded strings/values, native pairings and image hashes are unchanged. Neither fits. No equal complete decoded string of at least 100 UTF-8 bytes occurs across the inspected anchor/handoff/result bodies in either input. This bounded check is not a proof that no reversible encoding exists. Across-request rereads do repeat saved data, but dropping a later body changes that later input; archive availability is not identical immediate access. Machine dictionary encoding would also need model-readability evaluation.

**Semantic handoff:** a delegated read-only check confirms substantial overlapping meaning but no repeated complete strings within either prior handoff. Separator cleanup there saves only 82 / 81 bytes; repeated 32-character IDs occupy 640 / 608 bytes beyond their first occurrence before aliases. Most larger savings require consolidating wording or moving metadata behind refs. After JSON cleanup, retaining all other bodies leaves 9,221 / 3,594 incremental estimated units above an empty handoff-content schema. That is a capacity sensitivity, not a valid summary or a proven achievable fidelity target. No hand-written or generated smaller summary was substituted. Current/past pixel delivery, saved versus verified content, active/historical head, exact source/asset identities and unfinished cross-page relationships must not collapse into one vague caution.

**Agent-selected working evidence:** an offline production-policy projection explicitly releases the full saved-document read result and pins the immutable source version either as index or as exact `/content/blocks[8:12]`. It changes no stored policy. With original JSON formatting, the index alternative estimates 99,526 / 105,619; the exact four-block alternative 110,650 / 116,743. Both retain the complete pages 9–14 extraction message and all five/six native images, with no automatic additional deferral. Selected blocks equal the original values. These are analyst-chosen examples, not the model's decisions or proof that four blocks suffice for a faithful continuation. The agent must choose what it needs; full access remains available.

### Recommendation and decision boundary

The recommended single capability is **agent-visible working-evidence/capacity feedback with read-only previews of explicitly selected combinations**. Use the current request planner and revision controls; show which exact bodies, ranges and pixels are present versus summary/reference only, the saved head, and approximate component costs. Make recent replacement of complementary evidence visible. Let the agent preview its own release/pin/range choices and submit a normal revision without a human approval or a confirmation-only model call. The app must not choose the four-block example as a preset, force saving or page batches, downgrade full reads, or infer that supplied pixels were understood. Feedback must itself fit and describe the actual request; when no executable delivery route exists, report that rather than hiding evidence.

This is preferred over JSON cleanup alone because measured structural savings are insufficient. A smaller semantic handoff is a real alternative, but no equivalent candidate exists and the second cycle requires a much greater reduction. Capacity increases, tokenizer changes, timeout extensions and framework replacement are not established remedies by this comparison. The recommendation addresses a measured visibility/coavailability problem; it does not prove why the agent withheld another edit or guarantee that feedback will cause useful selection. Existing tools already permit these choices; decision support is the proposed missing capability.

If selected, acceptance should reuse both exact cycles and include the new diagnostic/tool cost; preserve native pairings, all originals, full access and exact selected values under both protocols; prove preview is read-only and only explicit admitted revisions alter policy. Exercise fitting/non-fitting/full-preserving selections and a real Runtime/mock-model flow through feedback, explicit choice, continued edit/save, reopen and export with actual source/Markdown/image/version/ZIP checks. An interruption must preserve receipts, policy, consumption and frozen requests. No forced method or confirmation-only call is added. True autonomous completion and fidelity still require separately authorized Stage B continuation, observing useful saved progress and interventions without forcing compaction or pin use.

No new renderer, save/reopen/export, live model or conversion-quality acceptance ran here; prior evidence remains applicable only within its original scope. The complete live task stays blocked with eight saved pages and six unfinished. The comparison's exit is reached; the user now chooses the remedy. No implementation, new broad review or live continuation follows automatically.

### Revised recommendation for discussion, not adoption

Improve **precise document access plus working-evidence decision support** as one connected proposed capability. Start with a clear model-facing document shape, actionable pointer/range errors and an optional exact-version block index (IDs, page references, sizes and full-read selectors). Explain root versus selected-body completeness. Feed the existing working-evidence/cost preview proposal with that navigable inventory. This follows the observed small-read attempt and feasible exact-range projections, without presuming a fixed block/page subset or adding a mandatory index step.

The reviewer's multi-batch retention proposal is worth keeping as an alternative, but automatic preference for evicting an unpinned document body is a substantive policy change: a saved body can still be immediately necessary for comparison, patching or cross-page repair. Full retrievability is not the same as simultaneous access. Both of the reviewer's final aggregate-pressure choices embed automatic selection; the third meaningful choice remains explicit agent-selected views. Do not adopt automatic document deferral merely because it fits or estimate its implementation by a line count. No framework replacement is justified by these findings.

Proportionate later acceptance: exercise the exact failed last-block read and corrected navigation; complete versus selected/ranged/index returns; explicit choices with actual diagnostic cost; both protocols and native pairings; fitting/non-fitting preview without mutation; and Runtime/scripted edit/save/reopen/export retaining old work. Keep the four recorded capacity controls, but distinguish preserving actually available evidence from newly fetching missing evidence. A separate live authorization is required to establish autonomous selection and useful new source-faithful output. Timeout changes are a separate continuation choice, not a prerequisite decision imposed by this review.

This reconciliation is complete. Findings and revised priorities are recorded; implementation, automatic aggregate deferral and another live continuation remain unselected. There is no automatic follow-on review or code change.

### Reviewer follow-up: precise reads and selection semantics

The user then supplied the reviewer's corrective response for assessment. It accepts the operation-count, causal, timeout and handoff-proof corrections and supports the proposed precise-document-access plus working-evidence-feedback increment. This is the reviewer's position, not a new human implementation approval. The direction remains reasonable with the following qualifications.

Successful precise access does not itself remove an earlier complete document body from history. A small read can replace the original acquisition choice prospectively; when a complete body is already retained, an explicit release or a separately defined projection transition is still needed. No automatic release follows from reading a range. A bounded in-memory check using the same read-only archive/production policy facade demonstrates this distinction:

| Existing pre-compaction state | Add exact `/content/blocks[11:12]` pin, keep prior full result | Explicitly release prior full result and pin that exact block | Images unchanged |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Before #55 | 140,408, does not fit | 102,077, fits | 5 |
| Before #58 | 146,501, does not fit | 108,170, fits | 6 |

These are existing release/pin projection costs, not a new `read_document` API's measured cost or a model-chosen action. No additional automatic deferrals occur. All 35 read archive inputs checked unchanged. The proposed index, new tool descriptions and diagnostic cost must be measured in their final native request; the reviewer's approximately 1.2KB index and 102K–106K combined estimate are not final implementation evidence. The #55 snapshot has five images and 8,805 units available in its no-handoff control; the six-image controls have about 3KB. Do not generalize all four controls as six-image inputs.

Completeness needs three distinct meanings: whether the original root is fully present; whether the JSON Pointer target is fully present; and whether the explicitly requested range was fully returned. For a 12-block array, `[11,12)` can be fully fulfilled while neither the whole array nor the root document is present. `[0,12)` and an unsliced array target cover the entire array, but still not the whole document root. `first == 0 and last == target_total` measures target coverage, not request fulfillment; compute `target_total` before slicing. Field names remain an implementation detail, but those meanings must not be conflated. Root objects and index views also need their applicable semantics, not a list-only formula.

A small source correction: `edit_document` exposes the `Edit` schema (`replace_blocks`, `operations`, `page_updates`, etc.), not the `Document` schema's top-level `blocks`/`pages`. This does not remove the documented need for navigable read shapes and actionable errors; read and edit forms need an understandable relationship, not identical schemas. Improved guidance makes recovery actionable but does not guarantee a model will never choose an invalid path.

The proposed increment and user decision remain unchanged. Its acceptance should include both a fresh precise acquisition and a precise reread while a full body already remains in history, without implicit release, as well as root/target/requested-range completeness. No new policy mechanism or further broad review is required to discuss authorization. Only this ongoing discussion record changed; no production/task/test/configuration change, commit, model call or live continuation occurred.

Official model information was consulted during the side discussion on 2026-10-27. The [GPT-6.1-Sol model page](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-6.1-sol) and [GPT-5.6-Sol model page](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.6-sol) list a 1,050,000-token native context window and 128,000-token maximum output. They also describe higher API rates for prompts exceeding 272,000 input tokens. That pricing boundary is not itself a hard context ceiling. These are official API specifications, not proof that the subscription connector exposes identical limits or accounting.

This is a dated observation of local Codex catalog fields, not a versioned project configuration or a Nexus capability declaration. The user confirmed that this Nexus route shares the Codex OpenAI subscription quota. Exhaustion can therefore stop both. The [official pricing explanation](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/pricing) distinguishes credits, included subscription usage and API-key billing; API price multipliers alone cannot establish this subscription's actual consumption or fees. That shared quota fact does not identify the cause of the later restored-quota timeout.

### Agreed direction and preserved requirements

1. **Establish capacity by model and route.** Record the source and confidence of effective input/context and output limits. Unknown routes may need a clearly labeled fallback. The inherited 131,072 default is not accepted as the permanent ceiling for this route or for other large-context models.
2. **Calibrate the quantity being compared with that capacity.** Use already retained successful requests and reported usage to assess text, image and protocol contributions before choosing a counting method and safety margin. Do not treat one observed ratio as a correction for every request or provider.
3. **Separate hard limits from operational targets.** A chosen compaction target for cost, latency, output quality or quota use needs its own rationale. A 272K API price boundary must not be presented as a hard model limit or automatically imposed on a subscription route.
4. **Investigate genuine overload as well as premature compaction.** Longer PDFs, dense page images, OCR alternatives, cross-page dependencies and growing editing history are useful design scenarios. They were not all observed as defects in this run. Correct counting or a larger window does not make context unlimited or establish that compaction and evidence selection are unnecessary.
5. **Preserve the conversion goal and agency.** Keep all 14 requested Nursing pages, faithful output, source originals, full/raw/field/range/native-pixel access, agent-selected representations and methods, effective policies, useful drafts, original receipts, consumption and frozen-request boundaries. Do not manufacture success through hidden reductions, automatic unpinning or a compulsory smaller evidence set.

### Options still requiring evaluation or selection

Capacity calibration and context-management design are connected investigations, not a requirement to add machinery before checking whether the observed pressure was avoidable. The recommended implementation priority should be reconsidered after that check; exploration of other-PDF overload can proceed conceptually alongside it.

- **Calibrated limits and counting:** likely to address avoidable compaction, but the actual Nexus limits, model-appropriate estimator and margins remain unverified. Neither 500K nor 1M has been adopted as an app setting.
- **Working-evidence and capacity feedback:** explain actual current bodies, images, refs, costs and headroom through existing feedback or an optional preview/dry-run capability. This may help the agent make informed choices. No new tool, required preview stage or confirmation-only model call is selected.
- **Precise reads and explicit view changes:** existing full/range/index choices and the main review's navigation improvements remain relevant. The main counterfactuals show local fit, not autonomous selection or sufficient evidence for faithful completion. Four blocks are not a prescribed working set.
- **Handoff representation or summarization changes:** lossless formatting alone was insufficient under the old baseline. A smaller semantic handoff remains an unverified alternative; preserving required information and useful progress would need evaluation.
- **Automatic retention/eviction or framework changes:** these are not adopted. Retrievability is not identical to simultaneous model access, and available evidence does not justify a framework replacement.

The current open question is which combination most directly improves autonomous delivery after distinguishing avoidable pressure from genuine overload. A preview tool should not be treated as the predetermined answer. Conversely, the lack of a new calibrated experiment is not a reason to dismiss management methods for other PDFs.

### Handoff, validation boundary and authority

This side discussion performed source/history and existing-record inspection, local metadata reads and official-document lookup. It did not run application tests, a tokenizer comparison, runtime replay, a renderer, save/reopen/export acceptance or a live model request. Main-conversation experiments and preservation results above retain their original attribution and scope. Neither new faithful pages nor a causal explanation for all stalled output have been established here.

The proposed next analysis is bounded: verify the route's capacity basis, use retained successful payloads/usage to assess estimation error, and reconsider the recorded evidence-coavailability cycles under justified assumptions. Compare genuine-overload options by agent autonomy, source fidelity, useful saved progress, interventions and whole-task resource cost. This describes a next decision/evidence need; it does not authorize implementation, probes, task resumption or a new conversion. Any live continuation requires separate applicable authorization.

This recording changes only this discussion file. The main conversation can subsequently reconcile the agreed direction with CURRENT_STATE, PHASE_PLAN and REVIEW_HANDOFF within its own authority. Their existing contents, product contracts, source, task settings, runtime data, other working changes and Git history are left intact. There is no automatic follow-on review or implementation batch.

Provider attribution means reported through this route; upstream model identity, billing accuracy, fees and subscription quota allocation are not established.

Every frozen request independently confirms Chat Completions, alias cliproxy/gpt-6.1-sol, BPE profile, vision enabled, configured capacity 131,072, output reserve 8,192 and image allowance 4,096. The input allowance is therefore 122,880. All route identity hashes are 323e4be385c75d06b2f8d34c06ebe6a077c3493c5871599bb232056973263d2c. This hash covers endpoint/model/protocol/parameters, not every setting. Frozen streaming waits differ: A 600/300/1200 seconds; B 600/300/900. No private connection configuration or credential field was inspected or printed.

### Three distinct measurements

1. Original estimate: the request's persisted meta.capacity, tied by SHA-256 to its actual frozen request_input.
2. Current recomputation: the current estimate() called on that exact decoded/re-encoded payload and that request's own frozen Route. No current SYSTEM, tools, source prompt or newer task state is assembled.
3. Provider input: prompt_tokens from that attempt's original SSE/native response. completion_tokens is output; cached_tokens is a prompt detail and reasoning_tokens a completion detail. Neither detail is added to input or total.

All 34 payloads are canonical byte-identical after decode/re-encode. Every recomputed estimator field matches its original recorded field, including text count, reserves, images, request/route hashes and fits. All original outcome_reserve values are zero. There is no hidden original-versus-current estimate difference to explain away.

The estimator, schema, working-context integration, Nexus, dependency manifest/lock and bundled vocabulary have unchanged Git content across A, B and current HEAD. context.py and tools.py differ in the FD-01 guidance increment; those changed prompts/descriptions are already in each run's own frozen payload. source-identities.json records the exact hashes. This analysis never substitutes today's prompt for A's prompt.

Current formula, with text proxy T and native image count I:

    input reservation = T + ceil(T / 4) + 1024 + I * 4096
    fits = input reservation + 8192 <= 131072

T counts complete canonical native JSON after replacing each native image object with a measurement placeholder. It uses pinned tiktoken 0.14.0, bundled hash-verified o200k_base ordinary BPE, in consecutive 8,192-Unicode-character chunks without truncation. This is a complete request proxy, not proof of route-native tokenization. The fixed/text/image terms are reservations, not measured protocol/image costs. Output reserve is separate. Unsupported modalities/configuration stop explicitly. Counter failure or explicit selection uses a visible UTF-8-byte fallback; no download or alias-based tokenizer inference occurs.

### Results

Relative error is (estimate - reported input) / reported input. Weighted error is the sum of signed errors divided by the sum of reported input tokens; it is not a fee or an independent-sample confidence interval.

### Error sources: observations, hypotheses and limits

**Text encoding/counting — supported observation.** In all six zero-image samples, the BPE text proxy alone exceeds total reported input by 547–5,493. This confirms a text/protocol projection mismatch on these samples before adding reserves. Complete serialized JSON contains field names, escaping, envelopes, repeated argument/result representations and placeholders that need not match upstream rendering. The data does not isolate encoding choice from protocol conversion or upstream accounting. An alias is not proof of o200k equivalence. Selecting cl100k or another encoding by a lower fitted error would not prove correctness; no alternate vocabulary was installed or downloaded.

**Chunk boundaries — supported small local effect.** Whole-string ordinary BPE on the same complete text yields 1–19 fewer proxy tokens per sample. With the same 25% reserve, the largest final reservation difference is 24; the sum is 416. All 34 fit classifications remain unchanged. This effect is much smaller than the observed errors. Whole-string counting gives up the existing protection against expensive unbroken/repetitive BPE inputs, without solving image or protocol accounting. No new latency benchmark or unseen-input bound is claimed.

**25% reserve — supported arithmetic, not causal decomposition.** It adds 258,389 units across sent requests: 30.61% of the aggregate observed gap of 844,092. Removing it leaves all 34 estimates high, with weighted error 39.63%. The gap also includes proxy and image mismatch; the reserve's exact necessary amount is not identified. Lowering this component is more defensible to validate first than altering pixels or using an aggregate divisor, because it preserves all content and leaves image/native/output reservations intact.

**1,024 fixed reserve — supported arithmetic.** It adds 34,816 units, 4.12% of the aggregate gap. Removing it changes neither retained compaction boundary from failing to fitting. A model-specific message/tool wrapper may still need a reserve. These samples do not identify its true fixed component.

**4,096 per native image — composition-sensitive and not independently calibrated.** It contributes 995,328 units across these repeatedly assembled inputs. The six zero-image observations cannot establish an image rule. Aggregate input minus text proxy mixes image cost, text mismatch and protocol differences; dividing that residual by image count would invent causal precision. Native images include source page renders, figure crops and document previews; details are unspecified in the payload and decoded dimensions differ. image_details in samples.json preserves dimensions, bytes and hashes without treating them as tokens.

The sensitivity-only 2,048 image allowance underestimates B #4 (:3:3) by 596 (24,507 versus 25,103) and B #7 (:6:6) by 2,051 (42,903 versus 44,954), despite retaining the current text and fixed reserves. Do not discard those samples or select an image rule based only on A's page-heavy fit. Conversely, unchanged 4,096 is not a proven upper bound for unseen crops, detail modes or routes.

**Protocol and upstream — unresolved.** All selected samples are streaming Chat Completions through one route and one source PDF. They expand the earlier non-streaming R01 evidence but do not calibrate Responses, another provider or every PDF. Provider usage is route-reported, not independently verified upstream metering. The historical 59-sample ratios 1.32–1.72 remain a distinct older population and were not used as correction factors.

### Concrete capacity and admission consequences

#### Actual frozen sends

All 34 were admitted under the unchanged estimator, with minimum local headroom 1,868 at A #10. Every smaller-reserve variant below preserves these 34 fit decisions. Therefore their average errors alone do not demonstrate a changed real execution.

The largest reported successful input is 75,940 at A #15 (:13:14), whose estimate is 112,022. The highest estimate is 121,012 at A #10, whose reported input is 67,925. Neither success measures the route maximum. Replacing the current counter with byte fallback would instead reject 20 of these saved, actually successful payloads at the same configured input allowance. This is a concrete restriction consequence of a documented fallback condition, not a request to disable fallback or change configuration.

#### Two retained natural-compaction boundaries

The saved context_compacted events record pre-handoff normal estimates 125,784 and 136,352, versus input allowance 122,880. These are the normal projections at handoff acceptance, not the frozen compaction payloads (66,521 and 73,558). The normal-before payloads were not frozen, so there is no byte-exact normal-before recomputation or corresponding provider usage.

For bounded sensitivity only, their recorded totals can be decomposed by the unchanged formula and retained native-image evidence. A had no context_update calls, pins, releases or recorded deferrals. The first preceding frozen normal input has 14 native images; its newly returned preview, retained in the next frozen normal, adds 1. The second has 10 and its five new retained render results add 5. Each boundary therefore has 15 native images (61,440 allowance). No Context method or task state was executed. Formula inversion yields text proxies 50,656 / 59,110 and text reserves 12,664 / 14,778. capacity-consequences.json records the inference and limitations explicitly.

| Independent saved boundary | Current | 12.5% text reserve | 0% text reserve | 0 fixed reserve |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Before :10:10 | 125,784 — fails | 119,452 — fits | 113,120 — fits | 124,760 — fails |
| Before :14:15 | 136,352 — fails | 128,963 — fails | 121,574 — fits | 135,328 — fails |

The text-reserve thresholds for local fit are approximately 19.27% and 2.21% at those separate boundaries, with images/fixed/output unchanged. This establishes that current reserve selection can determine whether historical normal content is locally admissible. It does not prove either normal request would be accepted upstream, that a compaction was unnecessary for quality/cost, or that an alternative history would eliminate two compactions. Changing the first boundary changes future agent actions and invalidates treating the second historical snapshot as that alternate future.

#### Run B's repeated full-result deferral

B #2–#7 defer the same immutable result, result:4d92481c49444ffe9351f914bd77c4e2:0:0:0, not six different results. Its saved size is 788,497 bytes; it is the complete two-page dict extraction. Four long strings are embedded image/mask Base64 data inside the textual dict (19,824 / 281,164 / 20,812 / 242,136 characters). It has no top-level image_ref, so this body is serialized as tool text rather than charged as native image pixels. That representation distinction matters; it does not authorize stripping fields or hiding source data.

Original before_admission_estimate values are 620,086–671,604, versus actual projected sends of 7,776–59,287. The recorded projection names that one result; native-image count is unchanged when its text body is deferred. Inverting each recorded full-input total gives local text proxies 495,249–510,249. Even sensitivity removal of text, fixed and image reserves leaves these proxies above 122,880. None of the assessed reserve-only alternatives reverses this deferral. Full pre-deferral native payloads and their provider usages are absent; these are local-proxy arithmetic statements, not provider-token lower bounds or proofs of actual upstream overflow.

The successful B task later chose text and native-pixel reads autonomously. That is inherited execution evidence, not a requirement to adopt a compulsory text-first path, an analyst-chosen field preset or a quality verdict against full dict access. Original full/raw/field/range/native access remains required.

### Options and tradeoffs

Sensitivity metrics below concern the 34 frozen sends, with the same complete text and images. They are not independently validated estimator replacements.

| Option | Observed undercounts | Signed error range | Weighted error | Sent fit changes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| current | 0 | 2,680 to 53,087 | 57.11% | 0 |
| text_reserve_12_5pct | 0 | 2,126 to 46,823 | 48.37% | 0 |
| text_reserve_0pct | 0 | 1,571 to 40,558 | 39.63% | 0 |
| fixed_reserve_0 | 0 | 1,656 to 52,063 | 54.76% | 0 |
| image_allowance_2048_sensitivity_only | 2 | -2,051 to 24,415 | 23.44% | 0 |
| whole_string_same_reserves | 0 | 2,679 to 53,073 | 57.08% | 0 |
| utf8_fallback | 0 | 17,828 to 172,826 | 220.85% | 20 |

| Meaningful choice | Fidelity/access, fit and fallback | Maintenance/portability and assessment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Retain current estimator/settings | Preserves all existing access and decisions; all selected sends overestimate, and two retained boundaries fail locally. Existing byte fallback is explicit but much more restrictive. | Small pinned offline mechanism; no route-wide bound. The recommendation to retain this calibration is withdrawn; unchanged software is an execution boundary only. [Component calibration](#recommendation-correction-and-component-calibration-proposal-2026-11-01) is the recommended improvement. |
| Route-scoped text reserve adjustment | Same exact content/counter/images/output. 12.5% has no sample undercounts and flips one retained boundary; zero percent flips both independent snapshots but is less cautious. Keep explicit fallback; do not silently apply a learned value to fallback or old frozen requests. | One component of the [joint text/fixed/image proposal](#recommendation-correction-and-component-calibration-proposal-2026-11-01); text-only reduction is not a complete remedy. A route/profile needs identity, scope, signed-error/held-out checks and reversion criteria. 12.5% remains one candidate, not a selected or universally adequate value. |
| Lower fixed reserve alone | No retained boundary reversal; leaves text/image mismatch. Does not affect complete access. | Smaller local gain and weak evidence of true fixed overhead; do not prioritize. |
| Lower per-image allowance / universal correction factor | Halving image allowance produces two observed undercounts. A universal divisor mixes protocols, images, schemas and reserves and has no evaluated bound. | Reject for current selection. Any future image rule needs applicable route/detail/preprocessing evidence and explicit unsupported-mode fallback. |
| Whole-string/alternate local tokenizer | Whole-string changes only 1–24 final units on sent samples; full payload and pixels remain available. Another encoding does not supply native/protocol counts. | Whole-string risks pathological counting cost; an alternate vocabulary/model mapping adds maintenance without applicable route evidence. No replacement is supported by this analysis. |
| Route-applicable complete-input counter | Could count native structure and modalities more directly if the exact route/protocol supports it; retain complete payloads, explicit unavailable behavior and offline fallback. | Official OpenAI Responses documentation describes an input-token counting endpoint for full Responses inputs, including images/tools and structural formatting. This Chat Completions Nexus route is not thereby supported; converting protocols would change the subject. It adds service dependence, request authority and latency. No endpoint was probed or called. A conditional future option, not a current replacement. |

Official documentation consulted on 2026-11-01: [Counting tokens](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/token-counting). It explains complete Responses-input counting and the limits of local plain-text tokenization. The inference that this route is not yet eligible follows from the absence of route-specific capability evidence, not an official statement about Nexus.

### Smallest missing evidence and planned validation

The measured restriction already supports prioritizing calibration; no further evidence is needed to withdraw the recommendation to keep it unchanged. Selecting and validating replacement reservations still needs the [joint procedure and discriminating evidence](#recommendation-correction-and-component-calibration-proposal-2026-11-01) below. A route-applicable count/usage for a byte-identified normal input near the first pressure boundary, with approximately fifteen native images and representative tool/draft history, is useful but does not alone identify text, fixed and image components. The historical normal-before bytes were not retained and cannot be invented; the two compaction responses supply no missing normal-input usage.

A count above a candidate reservation would reject that candidate; count/headroom consistent with it would support a bounded route-specific trial, not prove an unseen-input upper bound. Independent text-heavy/non-ASCII/tool-argument and image/preview compositions should then challenge the profile, because these 34 nested requests come from only one PDF and two successful tasks. Effective route capacity provenance belongs to CTX-01 separately and must not be replaced by a largest-success claim.

If subsequently authorized, validate a proposed component/profile offline against all retained requests first, preserving outliers and unknowns. Require exact payload/hash identity, signed errors by composition, unchanged full access and explicit fallback/applicability handling. Exercise counter-unavailable/invalid data and unsupported modalities locally, without silently changing capacity, output or image rules. Scope any new live interval separately; measure actual admission, source-led saved/reopened/exported fidelity, autonomous useful progress, compactions/retrieval/repair overhead and whole-task price-weighted cost only where fees are known. One changed local fit or fewer tokens is not actual agent benefit. Do not force compaction or continue either closed task as part of this plan.

The local analysis README gives the fresh-directory sequence: inspect_evidence.py, analyze.py, inspect_decisions.py, capacity_consequences.py, verify_analysis.py, verify_preservation.py and make_report.py, using the existing Python 3.12.14 / tiktoken 0.14.0 environment with -B. Each helper derives a fresh ignored output root from its own location; old writers/controls were inspected, not run. Catalog copies are read-only; immutable blobs are verified before use. No Store/Runtime/Engine/Nexus instance, Context assembly or task-state replay was executed. A socket audit guard records zero estimator-analysis network attempts.

Full per-request payload hashes, attempt/stream references and original raw evidence remain in the private local samples.csv/json, provenance.json and associated manifests. Repository-only readers can inspect the method/results above but cannot independently reproduce private-payload counts without those originals. No private source, credential or runtime file is added to versioned records.

The completed analysis independently verified all 34 raw SSE usages, unique start/request/attempt identities, source identity, CSV/formulas and complete metadata correspondence. Its selected before/after inventory covers 10,352 existing files / 633,160,512 bytes, with no changed/missing file or new actual-workspace file. Original catalogs, source, output trees, task/configuration and Git state, including DEVELOPMENT_GUIDE, were preserved. Provider calls, task replay, application/test/dependency changes, maintained-document writes and Git mutations were all zero under that earlier handoff-only scope. This later records-only instruction permits the maintained-document writes here, without changing those earlier evidence/scope facts or granting execution authority.
