## Recommendation correction and component calibration proposal (2026-11-01)

### Corrected conclusion and authority

The user challenged the recommendation to keep the current estimator/settings given weighted overestimation of 59.23% and 39.22%. The assistant withdraws **retain the unchanged estimator/settings as the preferred technical option**. Lack of implementation authority justified leaving software untouched; it did not establish that leaving its calibration unchanged was technically preferable. The earlier records-only instruction to keep settings described that execution boundary, not acceptance of the measured accuracy.

**Corrected recommendation:** reuse the existing BPE counting foundation and prioritize calibration of the whole-request estimate, with text, fixed and image allowances selected jointly from route/composition evidence. Neither the complete JSON proxy nor its 25% / 1,024 / 4,096 reservations are validated native counts. Lowering only text reserve is an informative sensitivity, not a complete remedy or a preselected 12.5% solution. Replacement of the tokenizer is not currently justified; improving the text/protocol projection or using an applicable native counter remains an alternative if reserve calibration cannot meet the selected objective.

The current user authorizes correction of maintained conclusions, a concrete calibration proposal and necessary offline arithmetic only. The application, estimator/configuration, route capacity/output allowance, compaction policy and task state remain unchanged. No implementation, dependency installation, provider/counting/probe call, replay or live validation is authorized. Nursing task closeout and deferred fourteen-page FD-01/captionless validation remain unchanged. This is a proposed method and proposed acceptance, not an adopted estimator contract.

### Calibration subject and loss function

Bind every observation to exact frozen native payload/hash, original estimate, input usage source, native-image parts and recorded route/settings. Keep all 34 existing samples, including both compactions, and retain unknowns rather than imputing zero. Do not count completion/reasoning tokens as input or add cached input details twice. Preserve original and recalculated fields separately. Future observations must use the same identity discipline; changing the prompt, native protocol, image preprocessing, model/parameters or tool definitions changes the subject.

Use the current complete native JSON text proxy T as the first comparison basis and assess this deliberately simple candidate family:

    E(r,F,A) = T + ceil(r*T) + F + sum(A_class(image))

Here r is additional text uncertainty; F is residual fixed/protocol uncertainty after T, not measured protocol cost; A_class is the complete allowance for one native image part, inclusive of its image uncertainty. Repeated native image parts count repeatedly. A scalar A is the first diagnostic family; additional classes need evidence. Output reserve stays separate. Configured 131072 capacity / 8192 output remain fixed comparison settings, not discovered limits. The proposal does not optimize compaction timing or reduce source content.

Do not identify r, F and A independently by allocating the same aggregate gap to each. On six zero-image requests T already exceeds total reported input by 547..5493. That supports examining zero additional text/fixed allowance; it does not prove zero protocol overhead or establish zero as a production value. Complete JSON T already counts wrapper/schema fields and escaping; a fixed reserve must cover supported residual uncertainty rather than blindly charge them a second time.

For each candidate report signed/relative error, undercount count and worst undercount, weighted overestimate by run and predeclared composition, maximum relative error, and exact local fit/admission consequences. Do not pool the older non-streaming population into one factor. Mean error and price-weighted task benefit are separate; fees remain unknown.

### Offline joint checks completed for this proposal

The new analysis-only helper reads the existing samples.json scalars and evaluates 100 predeclared combinations:

- r: 0%, 6.25%, 12.5%, 18.75%, 25%;
- F: 0, 256, 512, 1024;
- uniform A: 2048, 2560, 3072, 3584, 4096.

These are a coarse sensitivity ladder anchored to existing reserves, not supported model specifications, a continuous optimum or selected settings. No payload was reconstructed and no estimator implementation or task executor was run.

For fixed r and F, a conditional minimum uniform image allowance on the known samples is:

    A_min(r,F) = max over I>0 of max(0, ceil((U - T - ceil(r*T) - F) / I))

Also check zero-image samples separately: image allowance cannot repair a text-only undercount. U is corresponding reported input. This conditional requirement is an aggregate fit constraint, not independently measured image cost or an unseen-input bound.

| Text r | Fixed F | Smallest uniform A covering the 34 known inputs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 25% | 1024 | 2305 |
| 12.5% | 1024 | 2623 |
| 0% | 1024 | 2942 |
| 0% | 0 | 3070 |

All four constraints are binding at B :6:6, a mixed eight-image request. This explains why text/fixed reductions require image re-evaluation: keeping an image fit inferred under a larger text reserve would spend the same protection twice.

Among the 100 examined profiles with no observed undercount, the lowest aggregate error is r=0, F=0, A=3072: weighted overestimate 20.44% overall, 22.35% on A and 4.30% on B. Worst relative overestimate is 47.33%; the smallest positive signed error is only 18 tokens. This is a conditional in-sample result, not a recommendation to install those values. No examined zero-undercount profile meets the proposed 15%-per-run target below. This result does not rule out all uniform profiles or all other models; it shows that simply selecting one of these reserve reductions does not yet resolve composition-dependent accuracy.

With r=0/F=0, fitting only A yields A_min=2065. Applying it to B undercounts four of seven requests, worst -8038. Fitting B yields A_min=3070; transferring it to A has no undercount but still 22.31% weighted overestimate. These are diagnostic leave-one-run-out transfers; both runs were already inspected, share one PDF and have correlated histories, so neither is independent blind validation. Do not discard B's counterexamples or fit a separate profile merely by task/run ID.

Artifacts: .local/calibration-plan-20261101-r1/assess_candidates.py, candidate-grid.csv/json, conditional-requirements.json, transfer-checks.json and summary.json. The input SHA-256 and original branch/HEAD are saved in entry.json. These local calculations are reproducible using the existing Python environment and -B, with no dependency or provider access. The original 34-request analysis/evidence is not overwritten.

### How text and fixed reservations would be selected

1. **Keep the counter complete and explicit.** Initially retain the existing pinned vocabulary, complete JSON measurement, image placeholders and 8192-character chunking. Whole-string effects of only 1..24 final units do not explain the current errors. No truncation, mandatory field subset or source normalization is proposed.
2. **Separate length effects from envelope effects with zero-image controls.** Use existing zero-image evidence first. For future separately authorized counting/usage observations, hold route, message roles, tool schemas and generation parameters constant while varying complete text length and composition: ordinary English, non-ASCII, nested tool-argument/result JSON and long/repetitive strings. Separately vary message count/roles and tool/schema complexity while matching text count as closely as possible. Record actual T differences; approximate equality is not byte identity. These are calibration fixtures, not a compulsory converter workflow or matching-range reference answers.
3. **Measure the residual U-T.** Growth with T is evidence for a text-proportional residual; a stable residual with matched envelope evidence supports a fixed component. If residual varies with message/schema count, a single F is not a measured solution for arbitrary envelopes. Investigate a supported native protocol projection/count rather than inflate F to conceal a missing structure term. r and F remain empirical policy parameters unless the controls identify a physical contribution.
4. **Choose jointly, then challenge.** On the training portion, enumerate the declared r/F ladder and retain nondominated pairs that cover known text-only usages with lower excess and fewer parameters. With an image candidate fixed, apply the same test to complete mixed requests. Prefer the least complex pair that meets the selected error and holdout conditions; do not choose the largest residual separately for each component or multiply one worst ratio into a claimed bound. A tie remains a documented policy tradeoff until the held-out composition distinguishes it.
5. **Escalate the model on a specific failure.** If nonnegative reserves around complete JSON T cannot meet the accuracy target, improve the supported text/protocol counting projection or use an applicable complete-input counter. Keep all actual text, tool arguments, schemas and native fields represented. A new scale coefficient must not be fitted as a universal divisor or inferred from the alias. Compare it against the same complete payloads and declared route, with separate fit/validation data.

The present six text-only points do not separately identify a safe r and F for broader content. Zero reserves and 12.5% are candidates to test, not defaults justified by those points.

### How image allowances would be selected

1. **Prefer an applicable rule or counter.** Obtain documented evidence for the route's actual upstream model, image preprocessing/detail and complete-input counting support before using a model-specific image formula. A Nexus alias, MIME, byte length, pixel count or successful request is insufficient. The official Responses counter discussed above is not proven available on this Chat Completions route, and is not called in this task.
2. **Define scope using observable accounting inputs.** Partition only when supported by explicit detail mode and known preprocessing/dimension handling. Unspecified/auto detail remains a distinct uncertain scope. Source-page/crop/preview labels may help evaluation, but are not automatically accounting classes; do not fit by run ID or semantic purpose. Dimensions can select a validation stratum without being converted directly into token cost.
3. **Use matched incremental evidence.** Under future separate authority, compare a text/control payload with otherwise matched image payloads, keeping route, envelope/tool history and output settings fixed. Record added native parts and the actual T delta. The difference in reported input, adjusted for that measured text/envelope delta, is conditional incremental evidence for the changed image input. It is not a general per-image rule if other processing/routing changed. Challenge small/large images, detail variants and source renders/crops/previews, then mixed/repeated images at 1, 2, 8 and approximately 15 parts. These are discriminating cases, not agent tool quotas.
4. **Fit inclusive A values conditionally on r/F.** Use the full request inequality U <= E for all training samples. For one class, the A_min formula above is the feasibility floor on those samples, not a safe chosen value. For several classes, use each request's class-count vector and matched measurements; do not divide a mixed request's residual equally across classes. If the class-count equations cannot identify separate effects or a class lacks held-out coverage, do not claim separate calibrated costs. Add the smallest discriminating matched case or keep it explicitly uncalibrated.
5. **Reserve once and validate jointly.** Choose A_class values above the supported point/conditional requirements only to the extent justified by declared uncertainty and held-out residuals. Keep image uncertainty inside A, text-growth uncertainty in r and residual envelope uncertainty in F; validate the total so one observed gap is not used as three independent margins. Do not reduce r/F and retain an A fitted under the old larger reserves without rechecking every mixed input.

Current data supports rejecting a blind halving to 2048. It does not establish 2305, 2623, 3070 or 3072 as true image cost or safe replacement allowance. Newly supported image classes/native counting should be preferred over a large uniform allowance if they satisfy the complete-input validation below without sacrificing access.

### Selection and validation gates

The following thresholds are **proposed engineering acceptance targets for discussion**, not measured provider guarantees or adopted product requirements:

- No observed undercount on all retained known-usage samples and a separately held-out, predeclared validation set; retain every unknown usage with its denominator/coverage gap.
- Weighted overestimate at most 15% on each independent validation run, and at most 25% on each predeclared composition group with meaningful support. Report tiny groups explicitly rather than treating them as precise population estimates. These targets make improvement from 59.23%/39.22% concrete; another target needs an explicit product/risk rationale.
- A documented local capacity/admission improvement on unchanged meaningful inputs, with image/protocol applicability intact. Recompute exact frozen inputs; label unfrozen normal-boundary arithmetic separately. A local fit, these accuracy targets or a largest success does not prove upstream acceptance/maximum.

Use whole-run/source/composition partitions, not random neighboring requests. The two already inspected runs supply development and transfer diagnostics only; lock the candidate family/parameters/targets before new holdout observations. Use independent source/composition evidence covering short text with large schemas, long/nested text, text-only compaction-format inputs and mixed image/detail/preview history near the relevant pressure boundary. A 15-image normal input is particularly discriminating because no such request was sent in the 34-sample population. The historical unfrozen normal-before bytes cannot be invented. No every-provider/every-PDF matrix is required.

If no candidate meets the selected gates, document which composition fails and improve the applicable counting/image model or collect the smallest missing matched evidence. Do not silently relax targets, delete an outlier or select the unchanged baseline as technically adequate. A held-out undercount rejects that candidate for its declared scope; after using it for fitting, obtain a distinct validation case rather than claiming the revised fit is still held out. A bounded failed/inconclusive validation can close honestly; it does not authorize repeated calls until success.

Implementation and local mechanism checks would require separate authority. If later selected, all shared consumers must use the same versioned profile: normal/mandatory/batch admission, deferral, compaction segmentation/retention, handoff reduction and optional feedback. Preserve original frozen estimates/settings, accepted evidence, whole-batch settlement, full/raw/field/range/native access and the absence of a provider-token lower-bound proof. Unsupported modality/configuration remains explicit. Counter/data failure retains observable UTF-8 fallback; do not silently apply BPE calibration factors to that fallback.

Bind a calibrated profile to route/protocol/model/parameters, tokenizer/data and image-processing scope/version; keep unknown upstream identity explicit. Out-of-scope or changed accounting must expose an uncalibrated/fallback state rather than silently carry learned values across aliases, image modes or routes. The old formula may remain the named uncalibrated comparison/fallback mechanism where applicable, with its known restriction; that is not an accuracy endorsement or upper-bound claim. Do not auto-retune from usage or alter old task dispositions.

A later live interval needs its own finite shared allowance and stop/review conditions. Stop its calibration experiment on an undercount, route/usage mismatch or preservation failure; record evidence and decide the next discriminating step. Never resume either historical task as an implicit probe. Actual source-led saved/reopened/exported fidelity, autonomous useful progress, compaction/retrieval/repair overhead and price-weighted cost where fees are known remain the product checks. Compaction timing policy, route-capacity discovery, semantic handoff review, Stage D packaging and the deferred Nursing figure scopes are not prerequisites to this proposal and are not selected here.

### Present completion and remaining choice

This earlier task completed the recommendation correction, the 100-profile/transfer arithmetic and this concrete selection/validation proposal. No numeric r, F or A was selected for production. The later [ordered method discussion](#ordered-method-discussion-and-open-decisions) broadens the remaining choice before any calibration family or acceptance targets are adopted. Existing software/settings stay unchanged solely under the execution boundary; the inaccurate calibration is not endorsed.

### Proposal checks and limits

This documentation/proposal increment independently checks all 100 scalar grid profiles and six conditional image floors, preserves the original 34-sample table region, and passes repository-only documentation/source-integrity and Git whitespace checks (87 Markdown files, 4,445 local links/destinations, 503 bundled source files). File hashes confirm 9,567 selected original runtime/output files and all 37 registered original analysis artifacts unchanged. Nursing closeout, both original live-record bodies, FD-01 requirements, source/test/dependency files, settings and DEVELOPMENT_GUIDE bytes are preserved. Only fourteen maintained Markdown sources and isolated analysis-only helpers/artifacts are written. No application implementation, provider calls, task database/executor use, task replay or new live validation occurs; proposed parameters/targets remain unadopted. Local verification and input hashes are retained under .local/calibration-plan-20261101-r1/.

## Estimation method discussion and review reconciliation (2026-11-01)

### User requirements, terminology and decision status

The user challenged the incomplete method discussion and requested consideration of future Qwen, GLM, Kimi and other models, reusable libraries, existing coding harnesses and long-term maintenance cost. Multi-model access must not require manually maintaining an exact tokenizer/chat template for every model before it can be used. Preserve complete source/payload access and agent method choice. Support for a model and verified counting fidelity are distinct capabilities. Direct Chat Completions support must remain possible; investigation of Nexus complete-input counting is deliberately deferred to the last discussion question.

A tokenizer is a component that maps text to tokens; BPE is one tokenization algorithm, and o200k_base is a particular encoding. The current implementation already uses the pinned tiktoken 0.14.0 BPE counter and bundled, hash-verified vocabulary. This is a whole-request estimation/calibration problem, not an unimplemented migration from bytes to a tokenizer. Complete native JSON counting remains a proxy: its escaping, tool schemas and wrappers differ from an upstream rendered model input, while native images are charged separately. Neither a model alias nor a tokenizer identifies the deployed protocol/template/image pipeline.

Distinguish the expected input point estimate, a reservation for estimation uncertainty, and the separate output reserve. Existing metadata already separates several components; their numerical calibration is not established. High estimates are not justified merely by calling them safety margins, and low mean error does not justify removing every reserve. Both point accuracy and the final admission restriction need evaluation. Earlier 15%-per-run / 25%-per-composition targets remain proposals, not industry standards or adopted acceptance gates.

The assistant initially favored per-model counting profiles, then proposed shared estimation with usage anchoring and optional model-specific counters. After user objections and external reviews, that combination was explicitly returned to candidate status: its scope, replacement handling, shared-consumer integration and validation are incomplete. External agreement and sample-local success do not select it. Leaving the application/settings unchanged is execution scope only. The user's latest instruction selects recording followed by ordered discussion, not an estimator, numerical profile or implementation.

### Methods and responsibilities under discussion

Let P be an exact request, B(P) a declared local estimate, and U0 the provider-reported input usage of an identified earlier request P0. In all calculations state whether B includes reserves; the current reservation is T + ceil(T/4) + 1024 + 4096I for these recorded routes. Output remains separate.

| Candidate | Mechanism and expected value | Limits and maintenance implications |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Full local estimation | Measure the complete candidate request independently. Available without previous usage and for hypothetical/subset inputs. | Text/protocol and image bias can persist across long histories. Model assets may improve it but are not mandatory for every supported model. |
| Usage baseline plus eligible additions | Reuse U0 only when its priced content remains applicable; estimate actual newly transmitted content and uncertainty. | Needs defensible eligibility, image increments, dynamic-message handling and fallback. Prior output/reasoning totals are not automatically next-input tokens; count the representation actually replayed. |
| Residual correction | B(P) + [U0 - B(P0)], equivalently U0 + delta B. Can transfer an observed local bias to a related request. | Assumes the old bias transfers. It can undercount after additions, removals or replacements. A strict addition-only anchor is a restricted use of this idea, not an unrelated counting primitive. |

These are alternatives and possible combinations, not three independently selected services. A shared measurement/evidence contract could offer different applicable methods; which consumers may use which method remains open. Do not silently mix them at an admission boundary or label a proxy as an upstream bound.

Full local estimation is required by current internal workflows, not merely a first-turn fallback. Context.required measures SYSTEM/anchor/pins/tools without history or handoff; normal admission evaluates individual history groups during deferral; profitable_refs compares actual substitutions; compact_request changes instructions, tools, image inclusion and JSON representation; retention_target/handoff_target evaluate unsent continuations; apply_handoff compares assembled before/after inputs; inspect_context previews saved-policy changes. Those projections have no directly measured matching U0. A previous full normal usage cannot simply be assigned to them. Claims that other coding harnesses universally assemble only one request per turn were not established and are unnecessary to this project-specific conclusion.

### Existing harnesses and reusable counting components

The earlier read-only survey inspected the public source branches below on 2026-11-01. These observations are not claims about every released version, accuracy on our PDFs, or industry-mandated constants. No harness was installed or run.

| Source | Observed mechanism | Relevance and qualification |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [pi coding-agent compaction](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/blob/main/packages/coding-agent/src/core/compaction/compaction.ts) | Latest applicable response usage plus estimated trailing messages; full estimation when invalidated. This path uses text length / 4 and a fixed image estimate. | Demonstrates multi-model operation without exact local tokenizers everywhere. Its session/projection rules must not be copied without checking our request transformations. |
| [OpenCode compaction](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/dev/packages/core/src/session/compaction.ts), [text helper](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/blob/dev/packages/core/src/util/token.ts) | Separates measured and estimated portions; estimates new content, with text length / 4 and fixed media values in the inspected dev branch. | Measured history and uncertain additions can be separated; the constants are not validated for our inputs. |
| [Hermes usage anchor](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/agent/usage_anchor.py), [text estimate](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/agent/model_metadata.py), [image learning](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/blob/main/agent/image_token_cost.py) | Prefix fingerprints, usage plus appended-content estimates, CJK/UTF-8 heuristics, and model/host image-cost learning from usage residuals. | Useful patterns; a residual also contains text/protocol error, so learned averages do not isolate image cost or establish a bound. |
| [DeepSeek token-meter estimate](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/llm/token-meter/src/estimate.ts), [measurement](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/llm/token-meter/src/index.ts) | Fixed-density text/structure estimates and optional route image pricing. Measurement reuses usage only with a matching envelope and usage at least as large as the anchor's full estimated price. | That adoption condition would use no anchors in our 34 rows when compared with the current full estimate, or T + 4096I. This is a local comparison, not a result from running DeepSeek Harness. |

[Hugging Face Tokenizers](https://github.com/huggingface/tokenizers) and [Transformers chat templates](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main/en/chat_templating) can reuse model-published assets instead of hand-written tokenization. [LiteLLM token_counter](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/completion/token_usage) offers model selection/custom tokenizers but documents tiktoken fallback for unsupported models; a returned number is not proof of exact model support. Harness modules are coupled to their native message/session formats and are not necessarily drop-in Python dependencies. Asset/version mapping, fallback visibility and route validation remain costs. No package, exact per-model profile, automatic learning policy or universal o200k correction is selected.

### External review provenance and assessment

Three user-supplied reviews were reconciled against implementation/documentation HEAD 4a29ec3962da4f0582689753d6b5196a16d75371 and the existing scalar evidence. The first reviewed package review-bpe-20261101-153208-4e2e6a; relevant source/doc/sample bytes were checked against its ZIP. Subsequent reviews addressed the method discussion and returned reconciliations. Originals remain unchanged; local copies and hashes are in .local/estimator-discussion-record-20261101-dedbd30b/entry.json and external-review-1.txt through external-review-3.txt. Attachment IDs are 81387e2a-8492-4a82-9136-1313d88c4a07, 1097197c-9772-4340-ae17-d58878101a06 and 3fce94a8-242a-4e41-a89b-79524483c0a1. Their text is evidence to assess, not authority to execute its proposed checks or save its conclusions unchanged.

The original 34-send overestimation remains established: A 59.23%, B 39.22%, combined 57.11%. The 59 older non-streaming samples remain a separate workload; do not pool them into a homogeneous calibration/holdout population or universal divisor. During reconciliation, 100 grid profiles (1,300 scalar fields), six conditional floors, adjacent-request arithmetic and selected implementation behaviors were independently checked. This does not repeat the full application suite, original raw-SSE pairing or installed-build verification. Broad reviewer statements such as implementation integrity verified or all tests verified retain their actual supporting scope, not a proof of no defects.

#### Incremental image residuals and scope corrections

For the unadopted candidate E = T + 3072I, three Run B increments underpredict growth although complete-request errors remain positive:

| Transition by physical-send ordinal | (delta U - delta T) / added images | delta E - delta U | E(new) - U(new) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| B #3 to #4 | 3796 | -2172 | +851 |
| B #5 to #6 | 3491 | -419 | +598 |
| B #6 to #7 | 3362 | -580 | +18 |

This supports a material compensation/generalization concern: earlier excess estimates can hide deficient increments. It does not establish an individual image's actual token cost, a guaranteed failure for every short-history/large-image request, or a defect in the A_min arithmetic. The original proposal already labeled 3072 as an unadopted in-sample candidate, not a safe image allowance. Delta U - delta T includes changes in text/protocol estimation error; neither attributing all excess to JSON unescaping nor adjusting it with unrelated text-only steps isolates a physical image contribution.

There are 16, not the first review's 12, zero-image-delta normal-to-normal transitions (A 14, B 2). All have delta U - delta T in [-499, -63], with delta U/delta T about 0.819..0.994. There are 12 image-adding transitions. Eleven with smaller text deltas have residual/unscaled-32-pixel-patch ratios about 1.114..1.179, using ceil(width/32) times ceil(height/32); the omitted transition has delta T = 42764 and ratio about 0.793. Report the full population and exclusion rationale. This association motivates dimension-stratified investigation; it is not evidence of the upstream patch algorithm, measured per-image cost or a safe patch bound.

A negative delta E - delta U alone is not failure of a full-request estimator: its positive excess may shrink. It is directly consequential for a usage-plus-delta estimator that discards that earlier excess. These two evaluation purposes need different criteria.

#### Reproducible simplified-anchor comparisons

The following calculations use chronological observations separately per run. An anchor is used when adjacent sends are both normal, route_identity is equal, delta T >= 0 and delta I >= 0. Otherwise use the specified full fallback. This selects 28 transitions and six fallback rows; it does not verify complete message preservation, a proposed fingerprint or production eligibility. Current U is used only to score that row; the prediction uses previous U.

| Candidate | Anchored prediction | Fallback for six rows | Undercounts / minimum signed error | Maximum excess | Weighted error, all / A / B |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| Current full | None | T + ceil(T/4) + 1024 + 4096I on all 34 | 0 / +2680 | 53087 | 57.11% / 59.23% / 39.22% |
| Best examined uniform grid | None | T + 3072I on all 34 | 0 / +18 | 17225 | 20.44% / 22.35% / 4.30% |
| 3a | U0 + delta T + delta ceil(T/4) + 4096 delta I | Current full | 0 / +150 | 21024 | 12.51% / 12.91% / 9.17% |
| 3b, current fallback | U0 + delta T + 4096 delta I | Current full | 0 / +63 | 21024 | 10.16% / 10.57% / 6.69% |
| 3b, reduced fallback | U0 + delta T + 4096 delta I | T + 4096I | 0 / +63 | 17606 | 7.37% / 7.61% / 5.25% |
| 3c, current fallback | U0 + delta T + 3072 delta I | Current full | 3 / -2172 | 21024 | 7.38% / 8.09% / 1.45% |
| 3c, reduced fallback | U0 + delta T + 3072 delta I | T + 3072I | 3 / -2172 | 11462 | 4.18% / 4.67% / 0.01% |

Effect attribution must retain all steps: 57.11% to 12.51% changes to the simplified anchor with current increment/fallback; 12.51% to 10.16% also removes the incremental text reserve; 10.16% to 7.37% additionally removes text/fixed reserves from fallback rows. None is blind validation or evidence that a proposed block-based algorithm achieves the same values. The six fallback rows include first requests, compactions and the first normal request after each compaction. Their alternative reservations are analytical candidates, not setting changes.

The review's original same-state-revision gate contradicts these results: Runtime.tools increments revision after normal turns, and none of the 28 adjacent normal pairs has equal revision. Literal application would yield zero anchors. A handoff identity can help identify a generation, but is not sufficient to establish content preservation. The reviewer accepted this correction.

Image metadata adds bounded evidence: all 28 old sequences of (type, detail, sha256) are prefixes allowing equality of the next sequence; 16 are equal and 12 are proper/strict extensions. The later review's 28 strict prefixes claim is corrected. Deferred lists match on 27 transitions; the change is B #1 to #2, where the giant result first enters history and is immediately deferred. These fields do not verify text, pins, selected ranges or all native representation changes. Net token/image growth can conceal replacement, and checking release/defer flags alone is insufficient. Complete serialized JSON byte-prefix equality is not a useful eligibility definition for newly serialized requests; no measured message-level zero-eligibility conclusion follows from the scalar evidence.

#### Cross-state counterexamples and capacity consequences

Unconditional residual transfer produces the following results. N/C denote normal/compaction, and B_current includes current reserves:

| Transition | New reported input | New B_current | U0 + delta B_current | U0 + delta T + 4096 delta I |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| A #10 N to #11 C | 47080 | 66521 | 13434 | 12863 |
| A #11 C to #12 N | 9939 | 15508 | -3933 | 7089 |
| A #15 N to #16 C | 52534 | 73558 | 37476 | 36977 |
| A #16 C to #17 N | 21003 | 36926 | 15902 | 27324 |

These are genuine arithmetic counterexamples to unqualified transfer, not execution results from a replacement estimator. Let e(P) = B(P) - U(P). The residual-corrected error is e(P_new) - e(P_old): changing bias, not merely the sign of delta B, causes failure. Reject the review's universal claim that every negative adjustment is mathematically forbidden. Rejecting removals can be a conservative eligibility policy candidate; additions can still undercount, as 3c demonstrates. Provider image cost and particular text-escaping contributions remain unidentified.

At the two retained, unfrozen normal-before boundaries, U0 + delta B_current gives 67925 + (125784 - 121012) = 72697 and 75940 + (136352 - 112022) = 100270, both locally below the unchanged input allowance 122880. These are ledger-based hypothetical estimates, not frozen requests with measured usage or verified eligibility. They do not demonstrate two avoided compactions, an accepted route maximum, or whole-task savings; changing an earlier boundary can change subsequent history. The large Run B dict deferral remains an important full/subprojection case; an anchored sent-request comparison alone does not establish changed internal deferral behavior.

### Question 1 follow-up: image-area and responsibility reviews (2026-11-01)

**Recording agreement and status.** The user explicitly requests continuous recording whenever an issue is settled, to avoid loss of detail. Save the decision, its reasons, counterexamples, scope and remaining dependencies in this owner before proceeding to the next question; synchronize current summaries when their meaning changes. Record consequential review findings and unresolved disagreements as such. This authorizes records, not implementation or treating an external endorsement as the user's selection. Question 1 remains open; the two additional inline reviews recommend B, but the user supplied them for checking and has not selected A or B.

For this discussion, **A** means full local estimation governs capacity, with usage retained for observation and calibration. **B** means full local estimation remains independently available and eligible usage correction may govern capacity. Both require component calibration and visible uncertainty. B would revert to full estimation for an ineligible input; it also remains a design candidate that can be rejected if questions 2, 5 and 6 cannot establish useful eligibility, consistent comparisons and reproducibility at acceptable maintenance cost. Neither direction requires exact model-specific tokenizers as an entry condition. Broad unconditional residual transfer is contradicted by the existing examples.

The first new reviewer reports inspecting a ZIP at HEAD 4a29ec3, whose discussion owner ended before the later ordered questions. It explicitly did not review the estimator code or private payloads and assumed prefix preservation. The second reviewer proposes responsibility boundaries for B. These are two further reviews, separate from the three attachment reviews above. The reconciliation below uses current sources at HEAD 3776487701d3897ebc04a467f248e6f5d1a8fa6a and the unchanged 34-row scalar evidence; it does not claim a new frozen-payload eligibility audit.

#### Reproduced arithmetic and the new area candidate

The first review's 57.11% current error, 12.51% simplified-anchor error and 3.74% unconditional error reproduce. The unconditional variant has four undercounts, worst -33646 at A #10 to #11, or -71.47% of that response's reported input. Of the simplified 3a variant's 184892 excess tokens, 98731 (53.40%) occur on 12 image-adding transitions, 67453 (36.48%) on six fallback rows, and 18708 (10.12%) on 16 text-only transitions. This partitions **where** excess occurs across both runs; it does not identify 53.40% as image-caused error or describe Run B alone. The 28 anchor rows still use the simplified gate, not verified byte-identical history.

To check the new image-area claim, define M as the sum of native image width times height in megapixels, retaining repeated occurrences. For each of the 12 adjacent normal image-adding pairs, define y = delta U - delta T, x = delta M and i = delta image count. This mixed residual includes text/protocol differences; no individual image usage is measured. Use all 12 pairs, including A #12 to #13 with its large text addition; no outlier is discarded.

| Offline fit to the 12 transitions | Fitted coefficients | Residual RMS, tokens | Interpretation |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| y = a x + b i | a = 1180.38 tokens/MPx; b = -37.97 tokens/image | 212.85 | Reproduces approximately 1180 and 213; the negative coefficient is a fitted residual, not proposed negative image pricing. |
| y = a x | a = 1159.90 tokens/MPx | 217.89 | Similar fit without a per-image coefficient. |
| y = b i | b = 1928.34 tokens/image | 2554.12 | Area explains these mixed residuals much better than this declared flat fit. The review's flat RMS 2630 is not reproduced under this formula. |

The residual per added image ranges from 1127 to 3796. For the full-input candidate T + k M with no reserves, the smallest real-valued k that avoids negative residuals in these rows is 1117.47 on A and 1127.31 on B. The review's approximate 1117/1127 are rounded descriptions, not integer rates proven sufficient. These are sample-fitted thresholds, not route safety bounds. The largest single image is 5.429214 MPx, and recorded detail is unspecified. The existing 4096 term is an allowance rather than a claim that each image actually costs 4096; the new residuals challenge its calibration, not that distinction.

| B to A | 1170.59 | 3.27% | 5.36% | 0 |

The latter must not be attributed solely to better image accounting. All four fixed-rate evaluations have zero undercounts on their observed rows. Every saved request still fits the unchanged capacity/output allowance under these candidates, so these rows show no changed admission decision. No area-based decision is asserted for the unfrozen pre-compaction boundaries, whose full image composition was not reconstructed here.

This supports adding a continuous area feature to question 3's candidates; the earlier uniform-image grid does not exhaust full-local methods. The family was proposed after examining both runs, so this cross-run check is not an independent, blind holdout. #### Responsibility proposals, corrections and question 1 disposition

- **Independent capability is not an admission floor.** The first review's phrase "full estimate as the independent floor" misstates the proposal. B(P) is neither a proved provider lower bound nor necessarily a required minimum reservation. Using max(B(P), corrected estimate) would retain the current overestimation whenever the correction is lower, defeating that intended capacity benefit. Retaining both numbers for evidence is different from applying that maximum. Which value governs admission is still a design choice.
- **A has a maintenance tradeoff, not a structural incompatibility with multiple models.** Full-local errors recur when the same history is priced again; the implementation does not add the previous estimate's error to the next estimate as accumulated state. A can use a shared approximation and optional route profiles/assets without exact per-model templates. Its fidelity and uncertainty still need validation. B can reduce repeated history bias when a measured input remains applicable, but does not make generic estimates of new content safe by itself, eliminate fallback calibration, or guarantee zero transferred bias after dynamic changes. The area result makes a better calibrated A credible enough to compare, rather than proving B necessary.
- **Keep measurement separate from historical selection.** The second review's proposal to keep full/subprojection measurement free of hidden database/latest-usage lookups is a useful candidate boundary. A reproducible local result also needs explicit counter/version/settings assumptions; "pure" should not conceal resource/fallback differences. An upper capacity-evaluation operation could receive an identified, immutable usage anchor and decide eligibility. Its exact module/API and the consumers allowed to use correction remain unselected. required(), group projections and compact_request() still need a valid independent estimate when no matching anchor exists.
- **Keep absolute admission distinct from relative content comparisons.** A full-local basis for relative comparisons and conditional correction for complete normal admission is one candidate. A common compatible anchor for both alternatives is another; its contribution cancels only when both estimates share the same anchor and formula. Neither automatically resolves a candidate losing eligibility, deferral-driven reassembly, or a handoff changing state. Do not present a mixed-method difference as token savings caused by content. Diagnostic comparisons between methods on the same payload remain useful. These are dependencies for question 5, not a settled blanket ban on comparing methods.
- **Preserve both values and their provenance when correction is evaluated.** Keeping B(P), the corrected value, anchor identity/usage and delta/reservation details would retain the evidence needed for diagnosis and later reconstruction. This is a recommended responsibility boundary, not implemented metadata. Computing both is not proved nearly free: full counting remains, and projection fingerprints, historical lookup/storage and feedback convergence can add work. Record eligibility/fallback reasons as well as the chosen value.
- **Use structural applicability, but define the compared object.** Exact equality of preserved components is a useful candidate safeguard. A complete serialized JSON object, including closing delimiters, is not normally a byte prefix of a newly serialized larger request. A message-array prefix or an invariant-core comparison is a different algorithm and must account for dynamic task anchors, feedback, pins, tool definitions and route semantics. Current Context.anchor and working-context feedback change transmitted content even without compaction. The review's literal whole-request prefix rule cannot inherit the 28-row availability. Local fixtures can test the future rule's behavior, not prove upstream token additivity or its real-request coverage. Detailed eligibility remains question 2.
- **Freezing and acceptance proposals need their own decisions.** Persisting anchor ID, payload hash, usage provenance and algorithm version, and selecting against only evidence available before preparation, are sound requirements to examine in question 6. A pure function of an event-log prefix is one possible design, not proof that current events already contain every required projection/version/input. Reopen, preview and historical reconstruction need separate semantics. Splitting delta error, first/fallback-request error and actual anchor availability is useful for question 7; availability is not yet verified at 28/34. Zero observed undercounts should be reported, but cannot be promoted to a future safety bound or the sole acceptance criterion without evaluating magnitude, uncertainty, avoidable rejection and capacity behavior.

**Current discussion outcome:** record the area candidate and the responsibility corrections; do not adopt the reviews' recommendation to move directly to question 2. The assistant recommends preserving independent full measurement and traceable correction evidence as common design principles, while comparing A and conditional B on the same calibrated component basis. B remains worth specifying because valid measured history can reduce dependence on repeated local estimates; its advantage over calibrated A is not yet established. The user's A/B direction, numerical reserves, eligibility rules and acceptance thresholds remain open. Later-question dependencies are retained here without selecting them in advance. This follow-up changes no code, settings, source access, agent method choice, task/output/consumption state, Nursing closeout or deferred scope.

Conversely, 1.15–1.18 per raw area unit corresponds to 1123.05–1152.34 tokens/MPx. The earlier scalar patch check used sums of ceil(width/32) times ceil(height/32); these include image-edge rounding and are not identical to width times height / 1024. The two feature families are related hypotheses, not numerically equivalent fits or an established upstream patch implementation. ### Unresolved specification boundaries

The assistant and reviewers agree that the method must handle non-append-only assembly. Context.anchor includes changing head, delivery, notes and policy references. Pins precede history, historical arguments/results can be projected differently, and tail/control messages change. Working context feedback contains the estimate itself. Preserving some image hashes or reference IDs does not establish preservation of the priced request.

The later review proposes an invariant route/system/tools/handoff core, byte-identical old history/pins, separately estimated new parts, and explicit dynamic anchor/feedback replacement. Retain this as an unvalidated candidate, with the following open issues:

- Specify ordered, repeated native representations and their accounting metadata, not just sets or object IDs. Image bytes alone do not capture detail/processing changes. route_identity covers endpoint/model/protocol/parameters, not every estimator setting/version; define compatibility explicitly.
- Define the replacement formula. Absolute proxy difference can be zero for different texts with different upstream costs. Charging new replacement content or an empirical reserve is an option, not a demonstrated upper bound. The proposed feedback delta and replacement reserve are not yet algorithms.
- Separately tokenizing new blocks is not necessarily delta T of complete JSON: wrapping, boundaries and 8192-character chunk alignment can differ. Re-evaluate that exact algorithm rather than inherit 10.16% from another formula.
- Paired comparisons need compatible bases and explicit uncertainty. A possible common full-local comparison is unselected. Keep relative reduction distinct from absolute admission. A release can add reference/control framing, so do not promise that every release decreases input or bypass admission merely because a candidate removes some content.
- Mixed-method discontinuities are prospective integration risks, not existing anchor bugs. Context.admission compares policy deferrals, profitable_refs tests reductions, apply_handoff requires after < before, and inspect_context reports input_delta. Reviewer examples such as 72000 to 105000 or 72697 versus 73059 are hypothetical, not observed failures. Do not mistake switching methods/reserves for changing content.
- Freeze the chosen anchor request/payload/usage identity, its availability at preparation, counting version and reservation basis. Reopening or previewing an old request must not silently use newer usage, including that request's own later response. This is a specification need, not an implemented metadata change.
- inspect_context reproduces the saved normal input and checks exact encoded equality before preview. Changing an estimate changes embedded feedback; define historical reconstruction and feedback convergence explicitly. A shared estimator cannot silently gain mutable latest-usage state that changes the same saved input's historical meaning.

## Review of the outer JSON projection (2026-11-04)

The supplied review asks whether outer JSON escaping accounts for most of the high input reservations and whether the text projection should be specified before tuning allowances. The reviewer reports 37 passing focused tests in 64 seconds and three synthetic escaping examples. The attachment contains the narrative. Its exact checkout, scratch probe, input fixtures and test log are unavailable. The two named test files declare 25 and 12 parametrized cases here. The named estimator, context, outcome, feedback and test files match between package reference 4a29ec3 and local review HEAD 150be6587d2db3c5c274b3dd22a1a0fd3cfe7e5b. The 37 passes and the timing belong to the external review. The review's Q1–Q6 labels describe its own checklist. At the time of this review the ordered discussion was on question 1.

### Matched offline comparison on the 34 frozen requests

Each measurement starts from the original request object verified by SHA-256 and byte length, its recorded image allowance and output reserve, and the matching response's reported input usage. Recounting the production measurement copy (native image placeholders, canonical JSON, ordinary BPE, 8192-character chunks) reproduces the stored text count and full reservation on all 34. A tool result can itself be a serialized JSON string and a tool call can contain authored JSON arguments. Their inner syntax, whitespace, backslashes and Unicode remain part of the string value after one outer parse.

The attachment describes a decoded projection without a precise framing convention. We made two explicit local choices. **Joined decoded values** keeps the canonical keys, punctuation, quote delimiters, scalar values and image placeholders. It substitutes each native string value's decoded body for its outer escaped body, then counts the complete joined stream with the original chunk rule. The resulting measurement stream need not be valid JSON. **Split decoded values** counts the same string bodies independently and counts the intervening key/framing runs separately. A matched **split escaped** control retains the outer escapes with the same splits. Inner strings are opaque in every variant. Changing text boundaries can change BPE merges. The checks preserve the original request and compare the same recorded usage and input allowance throughout.

| Text projection, with existing text, fixed and image reservations | Sum of text counts | Weighted reservation error overall | Run A | Run B |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Current joined canonical JSON | 1,033,495 | 57.11% | 59.23% | 39.22% |
| Joined decoded values | 1,009,517 | 55.08% | 57.09% | 38.12% |
| Split escaped control | 1,034,271 | 57.18% | 59.29% | 39.33% |
| Split decoded values | 1,010,324 | 55.15% | 57.15% | 38.23% |

Weighted reservation error is sum(estimate minus usage) / sum(usage). Joined decoding removes 23,978 local text tokens. Including the resulting change in the 25% text reserve reduces the total excess from 844,092 to 814,118, a reduction of 29,974 units or **3.55% of the original excess**. Every reservation in the table exceeds its recorded usage and all 34 fit the recorded capacity. These comparisons cover sent inputs. The effect on unsent pre-compaction boundaries and subsequent agent behavior remains unmeasured.

| Composition | Current text count minus usage | Joined decoded text count minus usage |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Four image-free normal requests | +547 to +668 | +497 to +559 |
| A #11 compaction | +5,317 | −243 |
| A #16 compaction | +5,493 | −233 |

The two text-only compactions account for 11,286 of the 23,978 removed text tokens. The four image-free normal inputs account for only 315. The remaining 12,377 occur on the 28 image-bearing requests, whose aggregate usage also includes image processing. The reviewer identified a measurable local representation cost and an especially relevant composition for validation. The claim that outer escaping explains most of the two runs' excess fails this comparison. The rest of the text/protocol/image discrepancy remains unidentified. The synthetic examples' “tokens the model sees” column lacks an upstream token reference, and the tool-argument example also compares a body with an envelope. A provider can render roles, schemas, arguments and content differently after parsing JSON. Recursive decoding of authored tool text would change the accounting subject.

### Dependence of component calibration on the text projection

At 25% text reserve and fixed 1024, the smallest uniform integer image allowance covering the 34 observed requests moves from 2305 to 2364 when using joined decoded values. The previous fit on the 12 image-adding transitions also changes. Regressing the mixed residual delta U minus delta T on delta megapixels and delta image count gives 1187.16 per MPx and −43.51 per image, compared with 1180.38 and −37.97 on the current text proxy. For the same simplified adjacent-normal rule, all 28 eligible pairs and six fallbacks retain their classifications. Variant 3a moves from 12.51% to 11.33% weighted error with zero reservation undercounts on these rows. These figures are sensitivity checks on the same correlated observations. The prior strict-prefix check still yields zero eligible normal pairs for that rule.

The review's sequencing advice is supported. Name and version the text projection used for an A/B comparison and evaluate both full requests and increments on that basis before choosing text, fixed or image reservations. The aggregate results support retaining outer decoding as a candidate. They leave the calibrated production projection, A versus conditional B, component parameters and acceptance thresholds open.

### Source and test reconciliation

The bundled ordinary counter initializes with tiktoken 0.14.0, 199,998 token IDs and zero special tokens. Its pattern and vocabulary digest match the installed upstream definition under a source comparison that never calls the upstream loader. Literal special-token spelling encodes as ordinary text. The named tests cover corrupt resources, known vectors, propagation of an unrelated RuntimeError and full counting of long arguments. The source also checks the installed version. The two files contain no dedicated wrong-version case. Tests exercise preservation of authored JSON and escape spellings and prospective/committed byte equality. Provider-accuracy checks for nested-JSON projections remain a distinct gap, and a future local regression fixture could establish only the declared projection's behavior. The current full/subprojection paths share estimate(), including the self-inclusive working-context feedback loop.

The agent-facing fallback label, pending-outcome placeholder and lone-surrogate results are recorded in the engineering owner. Prior findings on failed loads, non-content modality traversal and frozen-build smoke coverage retain their earlier dispositions. [Engineering findings](../reviews/RUNTIME_FINDINGS.md#r01-november-4-engineering-review)

The review reports 0.33 seconds for two million base64-like characters on its host. Workload bytes, cache state, included operations and timings for a real admission path are unavailable. The text cache holds at most 512 keys of up to 8192 Unicode characters, or 4,194,304 character positions. About 16 MiB is a possible four-byte-per-character buffer total before Python/cache overhead. It is neither a per-key size nor a process memory bound. Midstream edits can shift chunk alignments, while identical chunk strings at any position can hit the cache. The aggregate deferral loop can try successively smaller sets of candidate refs, producing quadratic candidate evaluations in that branch. Actual work also depends on serialization, hashing, feedback convergence (up to 12 iterations), pending-outcome measurement and cache hits. Run B's deferral makes the path relevant. Its latency and any impact on delivery remain unmeasured.

It verifies the original 34 hashes again after reading. The attachment copy and its hash are retained locally. The review reads immutable request files and uses in-memory calculations. Provider calls and task-store writes are both zero.

## Conditional usage and retained input (2026-11-04)

The follow-up review accepts the one-layer recount and the distinction between a decoded local projection and upstream rendering. It proposes using B as the direction to specify, contingent on a structural eligibility check. We used the existing 34 frozen inputs to make that check concrete. Conditional B is the working direction for the eligibility discussion. Independent full estimation supplies the comparison and fallback. Choosing a production estimator and granting it admission authority require further decisions and validation.

### Accounting for the decoded reservation

| Contribution to estimate minus reported input | Tokens | Share of 814,118 |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Sum of the per-request 25% reserves on decoded text | 252,393 | 31.00% |
| 34 fixed reserves of 1,024 | 34,816 | 4.28% |
| Text minus usage on the six image-free requests | +1,632 | 0.20% |
| Image-row remainder, 995,328 in image allowances minus the 470,051 total of usage less decoded text on those rows | 525,277 | 64.52% |

This identity requires no attribution assumption. Interpreting all of the last line as image overcharge assumes zero aggregate error in estimated non-image input on the image-bearing rows. The implied 470,051 divided by 243 image occurrences is 1,934.37 tokens per occurrence, or 1,116.00 per MPx over 421.194487 MPx. These are occurrence-weighted aggregate residuals. The earlier 1,180/1,187 coefficients were fits to changes across 12 transitions with a separate per-image term. Reused images and the shared PDF/route link these observations.

The four image-free normal residues of +497 to +559 across reported usage of 3,888 to 23,247 make a common offset plausible for that composition. The two compactions yield −243 and −233. Request kind, instructions, tools and representation vary together. A matched request pair differing only in its schemas would discriminate the tool-framing hypothesis better. The data support examining a signed offset in a *point estimate*. The size of an *uncertainty reserve* requires separate evidence about future error. As a sensitivity illustration, transferring +497 to +559 of non-image overestimation to each of the 28 image-bearing requests would put image overcharge between 509,625 and 511,361 tokens (62.60–62.81%). This interval follows the transfer assumption. Other non-image biases would give different answers.

### One specified eligibility check

Inputs are the two runs' frozen Chat Completions requests, the joined decoded projection above and the matched historical request/attempt usage already verified in the original assessment. A candidate uses the immediately preceding physical send within the task. It requires two normal requests with matching route identity, protocol, model, estimator profile, recorded settings and exact canonical non-message envelope, including tools. Both recorded pending-outcome reserves must be zero. Only the previous accepted response supplies usage. Its input count must be a positive integer with linked request/attempt identity, a compatible cached subset and consistent total if the corresponding fields exist. The current response is used only for scoring.

The system message must match. The task object and terminal working feedback must occupy the expected positions with the expected shapes. Only the task's current document head may change inside the task object. Every previous middle message, including any handoff, pins, native calls, results and image parts, must match the corresponding new message in order and in canonical bytes. The added suffix must be one assistant tool-call batch with its matching tool results in order and optional native image message. The helper also checks that the old ordered image metadata is retained. First requests, compaction boundaries, changes to old history and unrecognized layouts use full local estimation.

On an eligible request the helper counts one separate, joined-decoded array containing the entire new task message, the added batch and the entire new feedback message. It adds the previous reported input and 4,096 per newly added image. It gives no credit for the old task/feedback already covered by usage, including when the new task message is identical. This deliberately conservative handling of replacements has a cost. It uses neither the aggregate change in complete JSON text counts nor an invented token value for removed content. The primary variant adds no further incremental text reserve. Fallback uses the decoded full estimate with 25% text reserve, fixed 1,024 and 4,096 per image. The comparison retains the feedback bytes originally sent. Recomputing self-inclusive feedback under a future estimator would require a separate check.

| On the 34 frozen inputs | Weighted error | Undercounts | Smallest signed error |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Full decoded baseline with existing reserves | 55.08% | 0 | +2,619 |
| Eligible anchor, 4,096 per added image | 9.83% | 0 | +426 |
| Same anchor plus 25% on the new text block | 12.35% | 0 | +640 |
| Same anchor with 3,072 per added image, existing 4,096 on fallback | 7.06% | 3 | −1,784 |

The rule qualifies **28 of 28 adjacent normal pairs**, or **28 of 34 sends**. The 6 full estimates cover the two initial requests, two compactions and two first normals after compaction. With 4,096 per addition the 16 eligible pairs with unchanged images have 0.99% weighted error, the 12 image-adding pairs have 15.57%, and the six fallback rows have 38.14%. Under full estimation the 4,096 allowance applies to 243 occurrences. The candidate applies it to **40 additions plus 6 occurrences on fallback rows**. Previous reported input still includes the retained images. This is a reduction in the number of times the local image rule prices them. It establishes no billable savings. A shared area rule remains an option for A as well. As a same-sample comparison, full decoded text with the original text/fixed reserves and ceil(1180 times total MPx) has 21.37% weighted error and zero undercounts. Portability of either image rule needs independent compositions and routes.

The harder cases qualify the original #9-to-#10 illustration. On B #6 to #7, the block method estimates 46,849 against usage 44,954 with 4,096, and 44,801 (−153) with 3,072. B #4 and #6 also undercount with 3,072. A #12 and #17 fall back at 15,387 and 36,784 against usage 9,939 and 21,003. All 34 sends fit the recorded 122,880 input allowance under every tabulated alternative. Every eligible flat-anchor estimate is below its full counterpart, so the population does not exercise an upward admission veto. The earlier 72,697 and 100,270 are ledger arithmetic using a different rule at two unsent boundaries. They have neither a frozen block sequence for this test nor corresponding input usage.

The check meets the reviewer's proposed exploratory screen of broad coverage and zero undercounts on these inputs. For question 2, evaluate each prospective input against the same identified frozen anchor. If only a newly arriving result is represented by a reference, the retained middle can still match. In fact B #1 to #2 qualified with the new result already deferred. If a release or deferral rewrites a previously measured result, the first proposed rule falls back for that candidate and declines to subtract an unknown cost from aggregate usage. In-memory controls on A #2 to #3 rejected a removed retained message, changed tools, missing/zero/negative/Boolean input and a contradictory total or cached subset. A fabricated positive count of one with arithmetically consistent details passed these static checks. An attributable, accepted attempt, compatible usage semantics and availability at preparation are prerequisites. A suspicious measurement can fall back to the full estimate. ## Question 2 decision on removal and usage trust (2026-11-04)

The user selected the retained-content boundary for the first specification of conditional B and included removal handling in question 2. This is a specification decision. Production adoption, numerical reserves and usage-trust thresholds remain open.

The later [release accounting settlement](#release-accounting-settlement-and-anchor-lifecycle-2026-11-04) refines the original fallback decision below.

### First specification and removal evidence

1. For each candidate, compare its retained content with the identified frozen anchor. The two app-authored task/feedback positions have explicit replacement accounting. A newly arriving tool result can enter as a reference if the old priced content is preserved.
2. When admission requires consideration of removals, compare the baseline and removal candidates on the same full-local basis. If a candidate removes or rewrites previously measured content, use its complete full-local reservation for the final fit check. Compute eligibility per candidate even when a preceding candidate qualified. Use qualifying anchored estimates for complete candidates only under the agreed eligibility rules.
3. Preserve the original anchored and full estimates, the candidate's full estimate, its eligibility and reason, the actual replacements, method versions and the decision. Mark an ineligible post-removal anchor value as unavailable. A separately labeled shadow text-credit estimate can document a disagreement. These records can measure differences in local fit and the amount of content deferred. An unsent alternative still has unknown upstream usage and delivery outcome.
4. Specify text-only removal credit as a shadow comparison on the **net local change**. Use identical decoded counting rules and matched message framing before and after the edit. Let D be the old local text count minus the replacement count, including reference and control text charged to this edit. Shadow credit is f × max(0, D), with 0 ≤ f ≤ 1. If the replacement grows, account for its net increase. Count each replacement once. For example, 1,000 local tokens replaced by 250 give D = 750 and credit 600 at f = 0.8. If the upstream net saving is 500, the credit still overshoots by 100. Validation must measure how the two *net* changes relate. Removed images receive zero credit. Use the full fallback for the first specification.

A trigger for investigating text credit is a naturally occurring case in which an eligible original estimate fails, an actual removal invalidates its anchor, and the full fallback and shadow text-credit comparison disagree about which candidate fits. Collect the frozen identities and observed usage wherever a request was actually sent, along with evidence of additional deferral. At least one real release or deferral of **previously measured** content is needed to exhibit the path. Repeated independent cases, compatible routing and evidence of retained source access would be needed to validate a credit rule. Run B did defer a newly arriving result. None of the 34 frozen sends measures the proposed removal of previously priced content. The 72,697 and 100,270 numbers for the two historical compaction triggers came from an earlier ledger calculation. Their full structural eligibility and upstream usage remain unknown.

### What the apparent margin measures

The 16 eligible transitions with unchanged images show +426 to +519 tokens of reservation error. The 750–19,812 range in the review is the gross block charged, including the new task and feedback messages. An ablation counts the same appended message array twice under the joined-decoded projection, once with and once without those two messages. The difference includes local array framing, BPE merges and chunk effects.

| Transition | Gross charge | Appended batch alone | Increase in reported usage | Gross error |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| A #3 to #4 | 750 | 339 | 286 | +464 |
| A #8 to #9 | 6,843 | 6,389 | 6,332 | +511 |
| A #17 to #18 | 15,937 | 15,488 | 15,430 | +507 |
| A #1 to #2 | 19,812 | 19,418 | 19,359 | +453 |

Across all 16, recharging the two messages adds **380–456 local tokens**. The batch alone exceeds the observed net increase by **22–65**. The net increase also reflects any change in the transmitted task and feedback. The deliberate recharge accounts for most of the stable excess. Its apparent safety margin is incidental and supplies no allowance for unfamiliar large additions. A 2% error on A #13's 42,625-token gross block would be about 853 tokens. This illustrates exposure to size-dependent error. The actual row added an image and had +2,480 total error under the flat-4096 variant.

Question 3 must distinguish the point estimate, the treatment of replacement content, and a named uncertainty allowance for newly estimated input. Evaluate a size-dependent text term alongside uncertainty from control replacements and image handling. Preserve output reserve separately. The existing incremental 25% raises weighted error from 9.83% to 12.35% with the same zero observed undercounts. It consumes more observed headroom. The sample supplies no validated smaller percentage, positive minimum or maximum. Evaluate independent compositions and one-sided misses before selecting rates.

For question 3's first comparison, freeze a point estimate that identifies appended text, task/feedback changes and added images separately. Score the positive part of reported input minus that point estimate on independent requests. Compare a fixed allowance for control/framing uncertainty, a term proportional to newly estimated text, and their combination. Evaluate short and long additions separately, then check image-bearing increments and full fallbacks on their own bases. Charging the full new control messages can remain a conservative comparison arm if that allowance is visible. Evaluate the headroom consumed alongside any remaining misses. The 16 same-image rows can motivate these forms. They cannot set their numerical parameters.

### Scope of the comparison and the trust gate

The flat 4,096 comparison reuses a pre-existing allowance. The full decoded comparator has 55.08% weighted error and the eligible flat anchor has 9.83% with its declared fallback and incremental text rules. The structural rule was devised after inspecting these runs. The area coefficient near 1,180 was estimated from the same 12 image-adding pairs. The 6.20% anchored area result (4.80% on image additions) also includes a 25% incremental text reserve. The full area result is 21.37%. These are in-sample comparisons. The 28 qualifying pairs establish availability on this observed population. Availability under active pinning, releases, policy changes and deferral of retained history is unmeasured.

Assuming a common 500-token excess from text/control effects gives mixed added-image residuals of about 1,177 tokens/image on A #6 and 3,833 on B #4. These estimates change with the assumed text error and add no independent image measurement. The flat anchor invokes the image rule for 40 additions and six fallback-image occurrences, compared with 243 occurrences under the full estimator.

A loose plausibility screen can catch gross anomalies without estimating a model's exact tokens. Using the existing prior decoded text proxy (including small native image placeholders and excluding image allowances), a half-text screen accepts all 28 observed anchor inputs and rejects a fabricated input count of one on every candidate. The observed ratios span 0.886–2.396. If rejection is used only to block a downward adjustment, the full fallback reserves at least as much for that particular candidate by construction. A higher reservation still has unknown upstream error and can cause unnecessary deferral. Previous response provenance, attempt association, arithmetic consistency and compatible routing apply in either direction. The half-text fraction remains an unvalidated screening candidate.

For a future response check, freeze the expected input and the reservation before dispatch, then record reported input minus each. A positive difference against the reservation identifies a reported undercount. A large negative difference against the expected input can indicate overprediction or anomalously low reported usage. Either sign can justify examining the route and composition. A possible response is to suspend anchoring for subsequent requests on the affected task/route and use full estimation while investigating. Trigger, scope, reset and threshold remain to be specified. Consistent upstream bias can evade this check. For a predictor of the form old usage plus a local increment, subtracting 1,000 from both old and new reported usages leaves its prediction error unchanged. All 28 prior ratios also still pass the half-text screen in this sensitivity example. Independent evidence would be needed to identify that bias.

The bounded ablation and gate checks are in `.local/estimator-removal-review-20261104-f75894f3/check.py` and `checks.json`.
