It checks the existing eligibility results and the hashes of its analysis inputs. Provider calls and historical store writes were zero.

## Question 3 reserve shapes and cache accounting (2026-11-04)

The user corrected the shadow-credit definition to discount the decoded **net** local saving. The question 2 specification above now states the formula and identifies the residual risk after pricing the replacement exactly once. The discussion also establishes an accounting prerequisite for usage anchors and opens a comparison of request shape, text composition and boundary headroom.

### Route-declared input accounting

Declare and version how the **route and protocol actually used** report context input. Record the source fields, whether the main input count includes cache reads and writes, any disjoint buckets, their units, and the handling of absent or overlapping detail fields. Preserve raw usage alongside its normalized total and the declaration used at preparation. For inclusive reporting use the main total once. For a documented exclusive convention add each required disjoint component once. If route semantics are unknown or the components needed to normalize a particular response are unavailable, the full-local path supplies the reservation. A model alias or an OpenAI-compatible envelope alone cannot establish the convention. The existing interpreter treats recognized cached details as subsets. Reinterpreting a different convention would require a separately selected change.

On these 34 Chat Completions responses the recorded main field is prompt_tokens. The sum of its cached subsets is 59,008 across 24 nonzero reports and ten zeros. Those tokens are already represented in the 1,477,936 total reported input tokens. A documented example of another convention is Anthropic's native API, which reports uncached input, cache reads and cache writes as separate fields. Its documentation establishes the semantics of that API. An intervening proxy needs its own verified mapping. [Anthropic prompt caching](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching#tracking-cache-performance)

A scalar sensitivity check simulated a reporting error by withholding a share of each prior total and applying the previous half-text screen to the 28 anchors. All 28 passed with either 20% or 40% withheld. At 60% withheld 18 passed, at 75% two passed, and at 80% none passed. This exercises the screen in isolation. It uses hypothetical shares rather than the measured cache hits. Each anchored prediction would inherit the omitted part of its own prior input. The loss can grow with the cached prefix and can change between turns. A plausibility ratio can flag a gross anomaly but cannot determine which fields to add.

The user requires a recognized provider context-overflow error attributable to a route to disable anchoring for future preparations on that route. Use full estimation while the effective context capacity, output reservation, accounting declaration and prediction are reviewed. Preserve the error, route/profile version and frozen estimate. Distinguish explicit context overflow from an ambiguous HTTP failure, timeout, quota error or truncated output. An attributed overflow establishes a fit failure on that input. Its cause and the conditions to re-enable the route require separate evidence. This is specification work. The historical runs had no such intervention.

### What the observed error can distinguish

For the 16 eligible pairs with unchanged images, decoded appended-batch count minus the **net** reported usage change ranges from +22 to +65. Dividing by each local batch count gives **0.304% to 16.195%**. A #4 is +53 on 339 local tokens (15.63%). A #2 is +59 on 19,418 (0.304%). Fitting an intercept and slope descriptively gives about +53.37 tokens and +0.0207% of local batch size. This is consistent with a nearly flat absolute residual on these particular inputs. It supplies no upper bound on a proportional term. Control-message changes can also contribute to the net usage delta.

The four image-free full normal inputs overcount by 497–559. Tools, framing and other shape differences coincide, so the cause remains a hypothesis. The two full compaction inputs undercount by 243 and 233. A compaction-only fixed reserve of 243 is the smallest integer that covers both **if every other extra reserve is zero on those two rows**. A round 250 is a sensitivity candidate. A learned correction to a point estimate can be signed and shape-specific. A reserve for uncertainty should have a separately named, nonnegative amount. Image residuals still mix text and protocol effects with vision processing.

Use full normal, full compaction and anchored addition as request shapes. Compare ordinary prose, Han-script prose, authored JSON or code, encoded data and mixed text as **evaluation strata first**. ASCII describes an alphabet, whereas JSON describes structure and Base64 an encoding. The classes can overlap, and separating a string into runs may change BPE merges. Cross composition with short and long inputs within each stratum and with the target tokenizer/route. Include full strings and real mixed strings. A class-specific rate earns its extra maintenance only if it predicts one-sided misses on independent inputs and improves boundary decisions relative to a shared rule. Tokenizer mismatch can create proportional error. Templates can add fixed or per-message costs, and protocol conversion can depend on field structure.

### First look at the image term

The pinned still-image configuration uses 14-pixel patches, a spatial merge of two, a pre-padding patch budget of 16,384 and a side limit of 512 patches. The per-frame 4,096 setting belongs to the video path. We evaluated the published still-image resize calculation on saved width/height metadata. It takes the minimum of one, the area-limiting scale based on floor-divided patch dimensions, and both side-limiting scales. After integer resize, pad each edge to a multiple of 28 and multiply the two merged grid dimensions. The checkpoint's model source replaces the media placeholder's sequence occupation with the produced feature length. We calculated dimensions without running a vision encoder. [Vision processor](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K2.5/blob/4d01dfe0332d63057c186e0b262165819efb6611/kimi_k25_vision_processing.py), [resize arithmetic](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K2.5/blob/4d01dfe0332d63057c186e0b262165819efb6611/media_utils.py), [model feature placement](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K2.5/blob/4d01dfe0332d63057c186e0b262165819efb6611/modeling_kimi_k25.py)

For 41 distinct image hashes and 243 occurrences, the grid yields 1,312–4,160 visual positions per image and 535,610 across occurrences. The previous flat allowance charges 995,328 units on the same occurrences. Two image hashes exceed 4,096 positions, each appearing four times. The 2,217 × 1,455 image gives 4,160 after padding, and 2,226 × 2,439 resizes to 1,713 × 1,876 and gives 4,154. Padding can therefore take the merged grid above 4,096. For visibility the per-request artifact also gives rendered text + grid positions − one existing pad marker per image, e.g. 49,549 + 21,959 − 14 = 71,494 on A #10. These are checkpoint-specific geometry/sequence calculations. They neither measure the historical provider's image tokens nor establish fees, upstream capacity or a safe allowance for other routes. Match processing identity and dimensions before using such a rule.

### Reading the Kimi evidence

Applying ceil(2.5% of batch) alone would still leave shortfalls of 11, 7 and 33 on A #8, #9 and #18. This is an illustrative comparison without a fixed term. It supports evaluating a proportional component on this content. It cannot establish 2.5% as a Kimi-family rate. These increments also include any rendered change in the control messages. The +448 maximum pertains to these 16 pairs, rather than all 28 eligible transitions or a measured Kimi response. The 100,000-text-token history example is hypothetical.

Record the rendering assumption with its serving stack. The measured 2,973/3,092 declaration deltas belong to the explicitly selected JSON branch of the pinned HF template. The anyOf/$ref diagnosis concerns one formatter/wrapper. The relative size of a hosted TypeScript declaration and the JSON branch is unmeasured. A hosted alias can change independently of the checkpoint. Likewise the still-image grid is computable when the resize, caps, padding and merge rules match. The two 4,154/4,160 examples show why 4,096 cannot be a hard upper bound inferred from the grid. Total route usage still includes framing and any service-side processing. A matched usage observation can expose a bad assumption. An aggregate total on one request cannot establish image attribution, behavior near a resize threshold or a transferable safety margin.

The Chinese +27.27 per 1,000 result covers 25 long mixed provenance documents, over a range of −141.12 to +56.56. Long Han-dense extracted PDF material with real tables and an independent vision-target tokenizer would add coverage. Availability of B under active context management is also open. ### First-specification profile proposal

Treat the tiers as independent capabilities on one versioned route profile. A route may have documented input accounting and an unknown tokenizer, or published image arithmetic and unknown cache semantics. Profile identity needs to distinguish endpoint/model/protocol, effective serving/processing settings and the revisions supporting each accounting claim. Keep one full-local estimation path. Preserve complete text/image representations, visible fallbacks and explicit uncertainty.

| Capability | Recommended first scope | Fallback and maintenance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0. Protocol and input usage | Require a supported protocol to send. Represent input accounting as inclusive, documented disjoint components, or unknown. Normalize previous accepted usage once, retain the raw source and version, and require the existing eligibility/trust checks before anchoring. | Unknown semantics or missing required components select full-local estimation. A declared convention alone cannot make an edited history eligible or a suspect report trustworthy. |
| 1. Tokenizer family and serving stack | Allow optional family, revision, stack and template-branch metadata, including unknown. Keep rate selection separate. | Use a named, explicitly uncalibrated full/increment profile when no validated rate applies. The default rates are still a numerical choice. A model-family label supplies no accuracy guarantee. |
| 2. Native images | Require an explicit image rule for a vision input. The minimum usable rule is the configured per-image allowance with its calibration status. Allow a published, versioned processor rule to be bound separately once applicability is established. | For the current Nursing/FD-01 route, use the recorded allowance as the available fallback for specification comparisons. Its model alias supplies no evidence that it runs Kimi's processor. An image-bearing request without an applicable rule exposes the existing capacity-configuration limitation. Preserve the image and full source access. |
| 3. Exact text/template adapter | Defer mandatory runtime integration. Support optional reuse of a published tokenizer and matching template contract where measured benefit justifies it. | Full-local and incremental text measurement remain available with the declared proxy. An exact adapter can improve both; serving-stack differences still matter. |

The maintenance advantage of B comes from anchoring compatible retained history and pricing fewer repeated image occurrences locally. It depends on actual eligibility and recognized usage. Published processor arithmetic can remove the need to fit an image coefficient for that processor, while route matching and validation still cost work. Prefer task-relevant evidence before maintaining another adapter. For a processor around an image limit, compare matched text with distinct image dimensions on both sides of the relevant resize/padding transition when separately authorized usage evidence is available. Preserve unknown fees and upstream identity.

**Open scope choice for question 4.** The assistant recommends tier 0 and tier 2's declared allowance/fallback as first requirements, tier 1 as optional metadata, and processor-specific arithmetic or exact text/template adapters as optional capabilities. Whether a verified processor binding is required for a named route before the first implementation ships remains open. This discussion performs no additional downloads, installations or provider calls.

## Question 4 settlement and question 5 comparison semantics (2026-11-04)

The user accepts independent capabilities and settles the first-release specification scope. Tier 0 requires a supported protocol and an explicit usage-accounting state, including unknown. Anchor eligibility also requires attributable, compatible usage and preserved priced content. Unknown accounting uses full fallback. A confirmed context overflow disables anchoring for that route pending review. Tier 2 requires an image rule when sending native images. A declared allowance with calibration status and observed-size scope suffices for first release. Computed rules can follow per named route with verified processing. Tier 1 is optional descriptive tokenizer/serving-stack metadata. Tier 3 adapters are optional. A family label alone cannot activate a calibrated rate.

**Allowance status.** For the original text proxy, the largest mixed residual per added image was 3,796 on B #3 to #4. The 4,096 allowance exceeded it by 300, or 7.32% of the allowance (7.90% of the residual). With the decoded appended batch alone the corresponding residual is 3,797.67, leaving 7.28% of the allowance. Charging the gross decoded block including control messages produces a different residual (3,666.67). State the projection and denominator. A useful label is “12 observed image-adding transitions, zero exceedances, smallest observed slack about 7% on the specified residual measure.” These are mixed image/text residuals per addition on each transition. The roughly 1,934 per occurrence on the full decoded workload uses a different aggregate assumption. Neither value measures an individual image's cost.

**Observation range.** Record matching route, detail and processor scope, image count, dimensions, area and aspect ratio. The 41 distinct image hashes here span width 873–2,226 px, height 951–2,439 px, area 1,011,181–5,429,214 pixels and width/height about 0.753–1.545, with unspecified detail. These marginal extrema describe observed coverage; their Cartesian product is not a validated region. Label extrapolation outside the observed dimensions/aspect/processing scope as uncalibrated. Preserve native resolution and method choice and continue through ordinary capacity admission. Feedback should expose the actual rule, fallback status and full estimate, without implying that an image must be resized or that an out-of-range request is guaranteed to fit. No processor cap has been established for the live route. Extend coverage with separately obtained matched evidence, particularly on either side of a relevant resize transition.

**Default text uncertainty.** A stated provisional rate is a first-release requirement. The user proposes about 6% for newly estimated text on an eligible anchor with an unknown tokenizer. Use 6% as the working numerical candidate, with an explicit text basis, rounding, status and version before implementation. Exclude the measured history from that percentage. Keep full-fallback text parameters explicit under their own profile. Record the actually applied reservation/rate and any alternative shadow rate, basis and predicted fit. Promotion or reduction is an explicit versioned decision. A shadow rate has no admission effect. The existing application still uses its recorded estimator.

The 3% batch-only sensitivity covers all 16 same-image Kimi template increments in this sample, whereas 2.5% misses three. A signed point-offset fit may have a negative intercept. That does not imply a negative uncertainty reserve or prove a fixed bias from framing. The largest long mixed-Chinese transfer was +56.56 per 1,000 (5.66%); the shorter provenance set reached +75.28 per 1,000. A second independent vision tokenizer and long Han-dense extracted PDF text remain missing, as does availability under active retained-history edits. A 6% rate costs 1,200 on 20,000 locally counted new tokens. Applied to the previously examined gross new blocks with the original control-replacement charge held constant, it adds 45–2,558 on 28 rows, with zero changed fit decisions on the saved inputs. Before adding that reserve the full-minus-anchor gap ranged from 2,481 to 51,816; only two pairs exceeded 50,000. These are sample sensitivities, not general headroom gains or a validated transfer bound.

For a baseline and a candidate, evaluate both on the same full-local profile, tokenizer, image rule, capacity and output reserve for the **relative comparison**. Record the signed difference in full-local input reservation and, where useful, its text, image and uncertainty components. For the **absolute decision**, show each candidate's eligible applied method, reservation, headroom and fit. If removal invalidates an anchor, mark the candidate's anchored value unavailable with a reason. If the local comparison bases cannot be aligned, indicate that the content comparison is unavailable. Keep provider usage and actual savings distinct from either estimate.

### Review of zero-credit removals and cross-content ranking (2026-11-04)

The reviewer reproduced the A #10 bridge and challenged the earlier recommendation to use full-local reservations for both relative ranking and fallback admission. The fixture demonstrates an incentive problem: a smaller representation can receive a larger governing reservation when its anchor becomes ineligible. A fit reversal is possible near a threshold. Both sides of this fixture actually fit, and observed agent avoidance, extra deferral or compaction remains unmeasured. Labels expose the discontinuity without removing it.

**Zero-credit proposal.** The reviewer proposes carrying forward an applicable anchor for voluntary removals from a fitting baseline, retaining the charge for removed material and charging replacements and uncertainty. The accounting must start from the identified prior usage **plus every still-applicable intervening addition, control change, image charge and reserve**. Starting from prior usage plus the reference alone would lose the rest of the input. Recompute each candidate from an immutable anchor and identified pre-edit basis. Repeated previews must not accumulate reference charges. If eligibility depends on pre-edit fit, that state must be identical for preview and actual admission. A nonnegative extra charge can still push a previously fitting reservation over the limit. A zero-credit candidate cannot rescue an already non-fitting reservation under that accounting.

**Pinned-template check.** We rendered 14 independent single-image deletions from copies of the actual A #10 messages. Each lowered Kimi-K2.5 rendered text by six tokens before visual feature expansion. The exact reference substitution instead added 43 rendered text tokens. The first removed image had 1,312 geometry-derived positions replacing one marker already present. For this template/processor sensitivity the net sequence change is +43 − (1,312 − 1) = −1,268 positions. Historical route usage for the edit is unknown.

Kimi's render_content macro emits adjacent text parts without a mandatory delimiter. In a controlled user message with the same tool declarations and generation settings, three parts containing “im”, “p” and “ortant” rendered “important”. Deleting the middle part rendered “imortant” and increased the complete token count from 2,982 to 2,984, with no replacement charge. This disproves universal part-removal monotonicity even for the pinned template. The image observations establish a narrower case. Any exception needs an identified route, edit class, full accounting of changed framing/control messages, applicable anchor and uncertainty. Pure image-marker deletion, text substitution and changes of role, tool pairing or handoff are different transformations. Testing one template also leaves upstream routing and multimodal processing open. Retain full fallback as the first-specification rule for changes to already measured content. A narrowly defined image exception is a shadow candidate for a separate decision.

The fixture's +50 is a **net change in full decoded text** (49,509 to 49,559). After deleting the image part alone, the text count is 49,503, so introducing the reference into that particular representation adds 56. Using 50 as a gross replacement charge would already credit six units. The proposed 68,492 also adds a marginal 6% to a historical anchor baseline computed without that new rate. A prospective figure needs an explicit, consistent block and reserve basis. The original 68,439 is an estimate for the unedited request, distinct from its earlier response usage.

**Point comparisons.** Compare candidates on one declared point model with compatible versions and media treatment, then evaluate fit with the governing reservation. **Disposition.** These results strengthen the case for a conditional whole-unit zero-credit exception; the +2 example alone does not justify a blanket fallback. A more proportionate specification candidate is to carry the identified prior usage, existing eligible additions and uncertainty forward, charge all new references, control/feedback changes, any newly loaded material and a justified boundary/representation allowance, and grant zero credit for the removed unit. Re-evaluate fit after those charges, even if the original fit. Evaluate from an immutable pre-edit basis rather than accumulating charges across previews. Limit eligibility to identified operations with supported grouping, tool pairing and rendering. The app can change several parts when releasing one ref. The empirical maximum of the complete edit is not a per-join bound; a reserve should cover the **residual after the specified replacement/control charge**. Current results do not establish a number for that residual across routes. The 741 controls give positive evidence for the observed operations on this Kimi template; zero observed increases does not establish a universal zero limit.

For image release, exact per-image pricing is unnecessary when zero credit is taken. The weaker requirement is that total retained visual processing cannot expand unexpectedly, or that any such expansion is explicitly covered. Independently processed images with stable settings are one sufficient case. A route can redistribute an image budget, or the assembler can load other content. Check retained parts and any new charges. A pinned Kimi text render alone cannot establish the historical route's multimodal behavior. The proposed exception is strongest for an identified route and operation with this evidence. Full fallback also remains necessary when usage or the original anchor is invalid. The earlier first-specification settlement has not been superseded by a reviewer recommendation.

## Release accounting settlement and anchor lifecycle (2026-11-04)

**First-specification settlement.** For comparisons, report the content delta under a named point model and qualify uncalibrated costs, the reservation delta on a common basis, and the candidate's governing method, headroom and fit. Name the baseline for method gaps. A positive point delta should be visible when the projected reference and control text make the candidate larger. Preview follows admission eligibility. Handoff comparisons use a common full-local continuation basis.

For a voluntary whole-unit release from a fitting anchor, specify zero-credit anchoring for supported **route/edit combinations**. Carry forward the identified measured usage and all intervening charges. Keep the old unit's charge, add replacement, feedback, reassembly and newly loaded material once, apply explicit uncertainty, and re-check fit. A previously fitting input can cross the limit after these extra charges. Record evidence by edit kind. Argument, result and native-image cases have offline evidence for the tested Kimi template. Give the historical route a **provisional** status for these specific single-ref edits under the first specification. This is a declared admission-policy choice with route uncertainty. The historical route's observed usage covers its retained input but provides no measurement of these counterfactual releases. Verify cache semantics, the original fitting basis, retained content, unexpectedly reintroduced media and any changes outside the priced edit. Unsupported pins, compound edits, invalid anchors, unaccounted reassembly and deferral search following an already non-fitting anchor use full fallback. A supported edit does not preserve fit automatically. For full fallback an estimate equal to the allowance fits, so savings equal to the earlier 2,904 or 13,472 deficits would suffice on the corresponding ledger basis.

**Corrections and checked evidence.** On the 55 positive complete Kimi text deltas, the saved production net reservation change covers 54. A #13's argument edit has +14 Kimi tokens versus +12 local reservation units. The prior table's minimum five-unit deletion was expressed in Kimi tokens. Recomputing the local deletion-only reservation gives a minimum deletion saving of one local unit. Adding counts taken from different methods cannot prove the gross-charge claim. Calculating candidate reservation minus deletion-only reservation entirely on the production basis gives 21–540 units. It covers every net Kimi edit and every Kimi restoration in these data, with zero minimum slack against restoration. This comparison includes the production text margin and image accounting.

A further matched calculation explicitly prices the marginal restoration using the **joined decoded text** subject. For each of the same 178 app previews, count the complete candidate minus the deletion-only diagnostic locally, with a floor of zero. This gives 19–418 tokens. For the rate sensitivity add ceil(0.06 × (old block + charge)) minus ceil(0.06 × old block). No candidate added images relative to its deletion-only control. This text marginal is an offline charging convention; compound reassembly needs a check for newly loaded input before applying it generally.

| Comparison target | Misses with decoded charge alone | Misses with incremental 6% | Largest remaining shortfall with 6% |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Net Kimi edit, all 178 | 0 | 0 | 0 (minimum excess 5) |
| Kimi restoration after deletion, all 178 | 57 | 6 | 4 |
| Net Kimi edit, 123 previously measured refs on eligible anchors | 0 | 0 | 0 (minimum excess 6) |
| Kimi restoration after deletion, same 123 | 41 | 4 | 3 |

Zero credit covered all observed **net** increases under this convention. Coverage of the net edit can coexist with underpricing the replacement stage because the target text saved by deletion remains charged. A #2's result restoration added **414 after pure deletion**, while its complete preview shrank by **19,377** and its deferral list stayed the same. No separate item changed deferral status in that comparison. Of the sampled complete previews, 47/57 argument releases and 8/57 result releases grew; 0/64 image releases grew. These are useful reversals to report per candidate, not a universal 50-token minimum or a population frequency. Release representation size is a separate product observation. ### Question 6 opening: bind the estimate to preparation

Runtime.prepare already reuses an existing request key. For a new normal input it passes the saved context snapshot and capacity metadata to Nexus.freeze, which persists the payload and returns an existing frozen request unchanged on re-entry. inspect_context distinguishes the recorded capacity from remeasurement of the saved bytes under the current method. Before hypothetical policy previews it rebuilds the baseline normal input from the snapshot and checks exact encoded equality. [Preparation](../../src/pdfmd/runtime.py), [freeze](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py), [inspection](../../src/pdfmd/working_context.py)

Proposed first decision: select the anchor once from evidence available at preparation **before** self-inclusive feedback is finalized. Supply that immutable basis to every feedback iteration. Freeze the actual input bytes/hash and final decision together with (a) anchor request and payload hash, accepted response/attempt and usage evidence identity, raw accounting fields and normalized input as known then, or an explicit no-anchor reason; (b) route/accounting/profile, tokenizer, renderer, pricing and eligibility versions, active rates and any provisional status; and (c) pre-edit identity, priced changes, point/full/applied reservations, output allowance and eligibility reason. Keep final payload hashes and reconstruction metadata outside the self-referential prompt. A wall-clock timestamp or the latest usage event alone cannot identify the preparation-time evidence.

Reopening a frozen request should display its stored bytes and decision. An exact historical preview should reuse the frozen anchor and original versions and first reproduce those bytes, even if a later attempt reported different usage, the current request now has its own usage, or a later overflow disabled anchoring. Re-evaluate the edit's eligibility against that same anchor. When required historical inputs or versions are unavailable, keep the recorded input inspectable and mark exact historical preview unavailable. A separately labeled current-method analysis can still be useful. The open question is whether to adopt this freeze/reopen distinction as the lifecycle contract. Implementation and storage layout remain unselected.

## Anchor lifecycle review and validation entry (2026-11-04)

The user supplied attachment 139c165d-1ae4-4f60-b25e-f0462f8f4f99 for review. The reviewer endorses freezing the preparation-time basis and failing an exact historical preview closed. The following is the proposed first-specification lifecycle contract. Its logical fields can be reviewed independently of the eventual database layout.

**Replacement uncertainty.** Rechecking the previous 178 previews confirmed zero misses against net Kimi text growth. On restoration alone, 57 local decoded charges fell short at zero added rate (maximum 13). Six fell short with the provisional 6% rounding convention (maximum four). The latter six have local restoration charges of 19–48. The 57 unpadded misses span 19–413, and the largest miss is a 383-token local charge versus 396 Kimi restoration tokens after deleting 22,446 Kimi tokens. A constant framing error on short removed bodies is therefore too strong a conclusion. A small fixed term is a useful *shadow comparison for any future credit rule*. For illustration, 6% plus four units per preview covers these 178 restorations and costs 2,104 extra units in aggregate, compared with 3,728 at 16% alone. Both cover this sample; these sums across related alternatives do not establish a chosen rate, a population bound or whole-task savings. Zero credit preserved enough deletion slack here. The +414 A #2 restoration and the B #2 released result leaving the deferral list retain their distinct identities in the preceding section.

**Selection and evidence boundary.** Select once from a consistent committed catalog view at the start of the logical preparation, restricted to history applicable to the saved graph state. An eligible measurement needs the committed request-to-accepted-response link and matching accepted attempt, usage fields and evidence identity. Runtime acceptance validates retained evidence and settles the request pointer and attempt facts transactionally. A response blob written to staging, a streaming chunk with usage or a timestamp alone is insufficient. A streamed or recovered response may qualify once settlement completes before the selected read view. The new request's own later usage cannot qualify. Existing activity events do not implement this cutoff. [Acceptance transaction](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py)

A crash before freeze creates two distinct cases. If preparation had no durable consequence, a retry may begin a new logical preparation from a new committed view and recheck admission. It may select differently. If an admission or policy result has already been committed against a particular basis, persist or reference that basis with the first dependent durable decision and reuse it for the corresponding request. If exact restart equivalence is required even before that point, a durable preparation-basis receipt is necessary. A deterministic function of whatever is latest after restart cannot promise the original choice. Proposed default: bind at the first durable dependency and avoid a separate receipt for entirely uncommitted work. A restart after freeze always recovers the frozen request identity and decision.

**Logical record and placement.** Separate the selected historical evidence from the candidate's evaluation. Pass a read-only basis explicitly to assembly and every self-inclusive feedback iteration. Re-evaluate eligibility for each candidate against that basis; never reselect from mutable latest usage during assembly or preview. Suggested record groups are:

| Record group | What must remain auditable |
| --- | --- |
| Basis and authority | Request/payload identity and hash, task/history position, specific accepted response and attempt, settlement classification or resolution identity, usage source fields, normalized input, accounting declaration, and the preparation evidence identity. Record a typed absence/rejection reason on fallback. |
| Eligibility | Rule and canonicalization versions, old/new subjects, ordered and multiplicity-preserving manifests or reproducible references for system, tools, retained native messages and image sequence including detail/processing identity. Record the edit class, replaced controls, first failed check/position when applicable, and material gates skipped after a failure. Hashes require retained originals and rules for reconstructing what was compared. |
| Pricing and gates | Applied route/profile, tokenizer/vocabulary, renderer and image-rule versions, image allowance/status/observed scope, actual text basis and rate with provisional status, output reserve, capacity, and the applicable overflow-disable state/version or decision receipt. Record applied and shadow parameters distinctly. |
| Candidate decision | Local point and full reservation, eligible anchored value or unavailable reason, the governing method, pre-edit basis, replacement/control/image increments, uncertainty, headroom and fit, with final payload ref/hash. A digest or reference to an immutable manifest can carry detailed evidence without copying every hash into per-turn model feedback. |

Compute these values before freeze. The current caller passes meta to Nexus.freeze, which stores it when it creates the prepared request, then emits a preparation event. Thus request.meta.capacity is a plausible home for a compact decision and versioned evidence reference. Its current expansion into an event is a reason to avoid putting entire manifests or raw responses there. Preserve enough information with any earlier committed coordinator decision that already used the basis. [Preparation and re-entry](../../src/pdfmd/runtime.py), [request publication](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py)

**Reopen, later changes and legacy records.** Inspect the exact stored payload and its recorded decision on reopen. For a historical hypothetical change, reconstruct the baseline with the frozen job/state, basis and versions, verify exact encoded bytes and the decision under that basis, then evaluate the edit. If reproduction fails, keep the original inspectable and mark the historical preview unavailable. Label a current-method remeasurement separately. Later settlement revisions or route suspension govern eligibility on future preparations. Preserve the old decision and its provenance, even if recovery later reports a conflict. For the proposed first version, reject unresolved or incompatible evidence and fall back for resolved-historical classifications unless an explicit supported usage pathway is specified. Reclassification alone does not establish that a past measurement was false. Display “anchor not recorded: method predates anchoring” when the method version establishes that fact. Missing required provenance on an anchor-capable method is an integrity/availability failure, not a legacy default. No backfill into old frozen records is needed. [Historical classifications](../../src/pdfmd/history.py), [inspection and preview](../../src/pdfmd/working_context.py)

An accepted new measurement resets the numerical starting point. For a simple eligible append, error is prior reported-usage bias plus the local increment error and the selected uncertainty. The prior estimate is not recursively reused. This avoids a chain-length reset merely to clear accumulated *estimate* error. Shared reporting bias, route changes, earlier content/deferral decisions and feedback-dependent assembly can still affect future requests. Missing or invalid usage, incompatible history, compaction and confirmed overflow retain their invalidation rules.

**Question 7 opening.** The lifecycle proposal is concrete enough to define validation cases. Include usage committed just before versus after preparation, crash before/after the first durable dependency, accepted versus staged attempts, overflow/reclassification after freeze, missing manifests, legacy records and invalidated preview anchors. For active context management, measure anchor availability over all preparation opportunities *and* the candidate edits that can change eligibility. Separate pin changes, first-arriving deferrals, releases of previously measured content and fallback after failed admission. Record the reason, actual governing method, decision disagreements and retained source access at capacity boundaries. The two real runs do include deferral of a newly arriving result and later offline release previews. They supply no live measurement of B making those decisions under sustained active policy changes. Offline fixtures can establish lifecycle behavior; independent observed workload evidence is needed for real availability and delivery benefit. No live sampling or implementation follows from this discussion.

### Question 6 detail review: decision ownership and the cutoff (2026-11-04)

The user requested slower convergence where details remain unresolved. The supplied source review endorses the freeze/reopen contract and identifies an event-schema hazard. **Event identity.** Nexus.freeze currently constructs the request_prepared event as {"request": key, "kind": kind, **meta.get("capacity", {})}. Exercising this function with an in-memory store and hypothetical capacity fields request=earlier and kind=compaction produced an event identifying earlier/compaction for a frozen current/normal request. Nesting the same two fields under anchor preserved the event identity in the control. This is a reproduced extension hazard. It supplies no evidence of a corrupted historical request. Keep all new anchor decision fields under a versioned capacity.anchor namespace. Put the eligibility-rule and accounting-declaration versions beside the basis reference in the compact decision. Protect the event independently with an explicit allowlist of diagnostic fields or a nested capacity projection, with event identity assigned exclusively by the emitter. Nesting the new fields alone leaves the generic dictionary expansion exposed to future siblings. Preserve access to historical flat event fields where needed. The full manifest stays outside the event and model-facing feedback. The prepared request record precedes its diagnostic event and remains the authority if publication stops between them. [Freeze](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py), [diagnostic joins](../../src/pdfmd/diagnostics.py)

**Durability map.** Bind the basis to the first committed consequence that *uses* it. The same small basis reference can be reused where decisions actually share evidence. Different candidate evaluations still need their own outcomes.

| Path | Current record and consequence | Proposed handling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Capture/register | ContextRevisions validates selections and stores a context_proposal. Register records eligible_pending or supersession. These steps do not perform capacity admission. | Retain policy and subject provenance. No invented anchor decision on a capacity-independent proposal. |
| Applied or rejected revision | ContextUpdates.finish stages a context_decision_candidate. Its transaction publishes the settlement descriptor, context_commit, disposition and optional active policy. Recover checks the state basis and requires the commit for a v2 receipt. Some structural/instruction rejections have no capacity evaluation. | Bind capacity-dependent evaluations to the committed settlement. Link the exact active/candidate evaluations when both contributed. Reuse the committed result after a crash. A staged candidate alone is insufficient. |
| Waiting for reconsideration | ContextUpdates.wait stores a context_check, sometimes with capacity. A referenced compaction wait guides continuation; the proposal can remain eligible. | Record the evaluated basis/reason where relevant and distinguish the diagnostic from an applied policy. Preserve which check the state actually references. |
| Request preparation | Runtime.prepare passes caller-supplied meta to Nexus.freeze; the prepared request refers to immutable input. request_prepared is emitted afterward. | Freeze the decision/basis association with the request. Retry reuses the frozen record. An event is a diagnostic projection and may be absent. |
| Compaction and accepted handoff | compact_request returns the compaction request capacity plus the retained normal target in meta. apply_handoff stages the archive, checks before/after/target, then event_once writes context_compacted and an event_receipt together. Recovery uses the accepted receipt even if the graph checkpoint lagged. | Record the full-local method and relevant comparison/target basis at the appropriate decision. The compaction input itself is full-local. A handoff blob alone does not establish acceptance. The auxiliary receipt in this path has recovery significance distinct from the preparation event. |

[Proposal capture](../../src/pdfmd/context_revisions.py), [settlement and waits](../../src/pdfmd/context_updates.py), [handoff acceptance](../../src/pdfmd/context.py), [receipt transaction](../../src/pdfmd/storage.py). ### Question 6 review of HTTP failure and Continue (2026-11-04)

**Verified recovery path.** Freeze persists the request before emitting the preparation event. A crash can leave a usable frozen record without that event. The proposed nested `capacity.anchor` and explicit selection of preparation-event fields protect both identities. The request owns the frozen decision. Selection must read the relevant catalog evidence through the same pinned read transaction, paired with the graph-state identity. Record what that view showed, such as “no settled input usage visible for this attempt.” Later evidence can corroborate the observation, but today's mutable catalog cannot in general reproduce an earlier absence or establish why the evidence was absent. [Store](../../src/pdfmd/storage.py) and [request preparation](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py).

An HTTP failure after a send is a distinct case from a route suspension before freeze. Nexus records the HTTP status, an `http_error` outcome and unknown usage. It stops without consuming the error body. The gateway test even supplies a body that raises if iterated. Nexus refuses replay of the failed request. Explicit Continue records the failed request and attempts, advances the revision and returns to preparation. The failed reply contributes no accepted turn. The successor can differ in content and admission outcome. The 34 sends supply no frequency or repeated-overflow measurement. [Transport and settlement](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py), [gateway test](../../tests/engine/transport/test_request_deadline.py) and [Continue](../../src/pdfmd/runtime.py).

The offline invariant-core check uses the immediately preceding physical sample within the run. For otherwise matching normal requests it tests for accepted, attributable prior usage **before** comparing message layout. A failed predecessor therefore yields `previous_usage` even if the continuation message would pass a future layout check. Searching backwards through accepted history could retrieve the older basis X. That would be a different selection rule from the one scored on these runs. No production request currently uses a usage anchor.

**Recommended successor rule, pending agreement.** If a send admitted with X receives an unclassified HTTP failure, preserve its decision and make the explicit successor use full-local admission. Record the failed request/attempt, basis X, route-accounting generation and reason. Keep X ineligible on that task across restart or another failed Continue. A fresh accepted normal request with independently eligible usage can supply a new basis. A generic HTTP failure supplies insufficient evidence for a route-wide suspension. A confirmed overflow linked to the affected route generation warrants the separate cross-task suspension proposed in question 4. Failure evidence and the guard must become effective together or recovery must derive the guard before selecting an anchor. Current failure settlement writes request and attempt separately. Include a crash between those writes in the proposed anchored HTTP 400 / Continue fixture. Check original bytes, successor basis, and task-local versus route-wide effect. A frozen unsent request subject to a later dispatch prohibition needs a separate disposition. For a policy already committed before freeze, retain the receipt and link a new admission decision if its basis was revoked.

**Unresolved classification boundary.** The current transport supplies no automatic overflow signal from an HTTP status alone. Reading even part of an error body would change a deliberate transport and credential-handling contract. One candidate is an explicitly enabled route classifier with a bounded read under the existing deadline and an allowlist of documented machine codes. Persist only a normalized classification and its provenance. Unrecognized, truncated, malformed or timed-out classification must preserve the known HTTP failure and task-local guard. Another option keeps the existing no-read behavior and requires independent trusted confirmation for a route-wide suspension. A circuit breaker can react to an observed failure but cannot validate reported usage. The open policy choice is whether task-local suppression alone suffices to enable anchoring on a route with no trusted overflow signal. Question 6 stays open; the proposed classifications and lifecycle fixtures have not been implemented or exercised.

### Question 6 successor eligibility and decisive admissions (2026-11-04)

**Recovery correction.** A successful HTTP status can precede a provider failure inside the stream. The decoder can identify a generic provider error. An explicit failure event ordinarily produces a rejected request, while a stream ending without a complete terminal response can leave the outcome unknown. Neither observation establishes context overflow. HTTP error bodies remain unread. Success-stream bytes have their own retention contract even when their contents are excluded from user-facing errors. [Decoder](../../src/pdfmd/streaming.py) and [transport](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py).

The proposed task-local guard applies when selecting an anchor for a **new preparation after an attempted request**. Reconcile locally first. An in-flight row can describe ongoing work, a crash before dispatch, or a completed reply with staged acceptance evidence. Honor verifiable acceptance; conflicting evidence retains its existing stop. When an attempted request remains unresolved or ends in rejection or HTTP failure, a successor selector must not search past it to reuse X or an older basis. This extends the previous HTTP-only proposal. It creates no Continue or replay permission. A retry allowed by the frozen unknown-outcome policy still refers to the same request and basis. A later eligible accepted normal request can establish Y. Proposed crash fixtures should distinguish a live attempt, an interrupted attempt with no verified acceptance, and one with recoverable acceptance, including interruption between terminal request and attempt writes. [Settlement tests](../../tests/engine/execution/test_acceptance_settlement.py) and [continuation tests](../../tests/engine/execution/test_continuation.py).

**What shadow data can answer.** Retain both reservations on each exact candidate, including candidates changed or withheld before freeze. Distinguish both-fit, neither-fit, anchor-only fit and full-only fit. When full-local governs, actual sends can reveal errors against attributable usage and cases where an anchor would have vetoed a successful send. The anchor-only candidate that full-local withheld has no observed outcome. A subsequent compacted or deferred send answers a different question. All 34 historical sends fit under both methods. The two earlier unsent pressure points have neither frozen candidate payloads nor matched usage. Shadow evaluation therefore has diagnostic value but cannot validate the added admission authority. A permanent shadow-only rule for the current route would materially narrow question 1's conditional B direction.

**Unclassified failures and exposure.** Example: X admits a request that fails; a full-local successor succeeds and supplies Y; Y admits another request that fails in the same task. Suppressing X bounds reuse of that basis, not failed sends per task. Frozen retries and concurrent tasks add independent exposure. Even an accepted request can exceed its predicted reservation or return biased usage. Keep reservation misses, remote outcome, unknown fees, extra work and task completion distinct.

The reviewer proposes a route-generation suspension after repeated non-acceptance when only the anchor admitted the exact candidate. Call this a **failure after anchor-only admission**. Count distinct settled logical requests idempotently, attribute attempts separately, and keep unresolved attempts observable without counting them as confirmed failures. Errors in schemas, outages and interrupted connections can produce the same pattern. Suspension can cost useful admissions across other tasks. Two failures is an unvalidated threshold; scope, exposure denominator, concurrent admissions, window and reset remain open. No body parser is required to record the association. Whether a gateway preserves a diagnostic overflow code remains unknown.

**Recommendation for discussion.** Keep conditional B as the design direction. Separate ordinary route authority from an explicitly bounded trial in which the anchor can actually change a send decision. If an absolute exposure bound is required, define it on physical sends for which only the anchor allows dispatch, reserve the shared budget before dispatch, and include any permitted retry in that budget. A first trial could stop on the first unresolved or failed such send, confirmed overflow, or observed input above reservation, and leave the route on full-local admission pending review. Even a successful trial establishes only the outcomes observed. The open question is whether to specify this limited authority for a route without a trusted overflow signal. No operating threshold, trial, implementation or classifier has been selected.

### Question 6 trial scope, exhaustion and lineage (2026-11-04)

The external reviewer supports limiting anchor-only admission to a bounded trial on routes without a trusted overflow signal. This is a proposed boundary for the first specification. User adoption, trial execution and promotion to ordinary authority are separate decisions. A trial concerns a specific dispatch for which the eligible anchor fits and the full-local reservation for the **same payload and allowance** exceeds capacity. Shadow diagnostics may also show the opposite disagreement. Specify separately whether an anchor can veto a full-local fit during a trial. Off or suspended mode uses full-local admission in both directions.

| Reviewer condition | Reconciliation and proposed behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Task lockout after the first decisive failure | Reconcile staged acceptance first. On a failed or still-unresolved decisive attempt, stop new decisive dispatches on the trial and keep this task on full-local authority for its remainder, even after a fresh anchor or a later reopening of the trial. Preserve the frozen request. An existing replay permission cannot bypass the dispatch stop. Successful receipt with normalized input above reservation also stops the trial. If usage is missing or cannot be attributed, record acceptance separately and withhold a clean validation outcome pending review. |
| Budget and exhaustion | Give the trial a total physical-attempt budget N and a separate concurrency limit K. The existing task lock does not serialize different tasks. Recommend K = 1 for an initial trial, with a slot held until reconciliation and the stop decision are durable. Check task lockout, trial state, budget and slot at the physical dispatch boundary, including retries. An unresolved reserved slot after a crash cannot be quietly refunded. When the last slot is allocated, stop allocating; while its outcome is outstanding report pending settlement. On clean exhaustion, show **trial complete, review pending**, retain full-local authority and require explicit review for promotion. Distinguish a stop on failure from clean exhaustion. |
| Durable identity and lineage | The estimator's route hash covers endpoint, model, protocol and generation parameters. Capacity, output reserve, image allowance, estimator profile and wait policy are separate fields. Several changes that alter a decisive outcome or failure attribution leave the hash unchanged. Bind the trial evidence to a versioned configuration including the accounting declaration, eligibility and reservation rules. Give the budget and suspension their own explicitly identified trial lineage. A changed or unrecognized version has no authority to dispatch under a new budget. Associate versions explicitly on review; matching endpoint and model alone proves neither equivalent accounting nor a common upstream. Each Store has its own SQLite catalog, so a workspace-wide limit does not imply a limit across independent workspaces. No new table or cross-workspace coordinator has been selected. |
| Visible modes and defaults | Show the governing mode, trial identity, remaining budget, decisive attempts and stop/exhaustion reason. Recommend full-local authority by default and explicit enrollment for a named trial. A user can stop future decisive dispatches. A user switching a mode off cannot retract an already dispatched attempt. A general-purpose on switch would bypass the review boundary on a route without a trusted overflow signal. Routine authority would require its own decision. |

**Interpretation.** The 34 successful requests give zero observed anchor-only opportunities among the frozen sends examined. How often ordinary tasks reach that region remains unknown. Record the number of preparation opportunities, disagreements, attempts actually dispatched, and assessable outcomes separately. For 20 independent trials with a common failure probability and complete observation, zero failures gives an exact one-sided 95% upper limit of about 13.9% (one minus 0.05 raised to the power 1/20). The familiar 3/N approximation gives 15%. Sequential requests from the same conversion share content and route conditions. Neither figure describes this route's measured risk, establishes benefit or selects N. N limits total exposure and K limits simultaneous exposure; both affect risk as well as elapsed time.

**Decision still open.** Should anchor-only admission require explicit enrollment of a named route/configuration in a bounded trial, with full-local authority outside it? The recommendation is yes. Neither a reviewer endorsement nor the absence of disagreements on saved sends enables a trial. After deciding authority, settle the remaining dispatch, veto and evidence rules before moving to question 7. [Route fields](../../src/pdfmd/schema.py), [estimator fingerprint](../../src/pdfmd/request_estimation.py), [per-task locking](../../src/pdfmd/runtime.py), [attempt transaction and acceptance recovery](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py), [workspace catalog](../../src/pdfmd/storage.py).

### Question 6 two-way authority and veto exposure (2026-11-04)

**Working specification.** The latest reconciliation withdraws the outside-trial veto. Off, suspension and task lockout leave full-local admission authoritative in both directions.
