### Question 6 first-veto disposition (2026-11-04)

The first authoritative veto is the review trigger for the working trial specification. Withhold that exact normal candidate, record the decision and pause further trial authority. The reviewer proposes automatically sending a compaction request as the consequence. That additional action remains open. Pausing trial authority and holding a conversion for review are separate decisions.

**Why ordinary deferral does not resolve this disagreement.** A veto means the anchor fails and full-local fits on the same complete candidate at the same allowance. The existing normal assembler searches for deferrals only after its complete local reservation fails. An individually fitting group is returned unchanged. Asking full-local whether the unchanged candidate needs deferral therefore selects no removal. The first specification gives no authority to evict an otherwise fitting item just to manufacture a different candidate. Zero credit for removing already measured content cannot create the missing headroom. A smaller, genuinely changed successor can be evaluated under its own applicable rules. Simply resuming the identical candidate under the now-paused trial's full-local mode would erase the veto. [Normal projection and deferral](../../src/pdfmd/context.py)

**A veto is observable without a compaction.** The current continuation path sends a fitting full-local normal request. It has no anchor-veto branch. For a nonfitting normal it checks required content, can stop, and otherwise builds a separate compaction request. That request can itself lack a fitting segment. Preparing or sending one does not establish that the agent produced a valid handoff. Acceptance checks revision, covered history, reduction and applicable continuation conditions. An accepted handoff changes the active history and retains original references and the archive chain; a failed or rejected handoff does not establish that transition. [Continuation selection](../../src/pdfmd/context_updates.py), [handoff checks](../../src/pdfmd/context.py), [runtime](../../src/pdfmd/runtime.py).

For an initial estimator trial, recommend a durable hold on the affected conversion **before any veto-induced compaction dispatch**. Preserve the candidate identity and governing basis, the veto reason, trial suspension and task disposition as a recoverable decision. Restart must not turn it into an automatic full-local send. An explicit reviewed release of the hold could permit a fresh assessment, including recorded override if the identical payload is allowed. The hold interrupts progress and does not measure what a hands-off compaction policy would have achieved. Alternatively, enrolling a task could explicitly allow the ordinary compaction path after this veto, subject to its independent fit, budget, response and acceptance checks. This can let conversion continue but exposes it to another send and a different active history. If chosen, link the veto to separate outcomes for compaction considered, prepared, dispatched, failed or handoff accepted. Count actual deferrals if any. Do not populate a compaction count at veto time. Charging vetoes against a send budget would mix different exposures. Subtraction-based deferral would reopen question 2.

**Feedback and reproduction.** Off, suspended or ineligible requests retain the applicable full-local feedback. An enrolled eligible normal candidate can be assembled with the governing anchor basis frozen, using a self-inclusive reservation with the correct method label. The current assembler iterates the feedback value in the payload and normal admission can also reserve for a pending context outcome. Do not substitute a final ledger number into already-measured bytes. Compare both methods on the very same finalized candidate, including the same pending-outcome treatment. A withheld candidate sends no feedback. Keep shadow-only fields out of model-visible channels. Reading frozen bytes requires no shadow state. Reproducing a hypothetical preview additionally requires the saved assembly basis and matching the original bytes. Shadow isolation removes one dependency; byte equality and the governing decision still require separate checks. [Feedback and snapshot preview](../../src/pdfmd/working_context.py)

The three flat/area ordering reversals establish neither an expected early veto nor its cause on a live route. There was no veto at the recorded allowance, including against production. If a future trial uses the existing allowance, label the studied range and freeze the baseline. An independently evaluated alternative image term would test a different profile. Choosing an image coefficient is unnecessary for settling the first-veto disposition, and neither choice authorizes enrollment. Question 7 should link preparation opportunities, dispatches, vetoes and actual dispositions. Track attributable usage against both reservations only for exact sent payloads, alongside the shadow-equivalence and restart checks.

### Question 6 first-veto override and payload identity (2026-11-04)

**First-trial disposition.** Record C as the default for an explicitly enrolled trial on a route without a trusted overflow signal. At the first eligible upward fit disagreement, persist the trial's veto decision and suspension. Full-local admission can override the veto for ordinary dispatch of the exact fitting candidate. A durable hold (A) is an explicit alternative chosen at enrollment. The veto alone cannot initiate compaction (B) in this trial. Preserve ordinary checks for genuinely changed future inputs. Suspension governs subsequent decisions. It neither erases the veto nor logically requires a send or a hold on the earlier candidate. The recorded disposition supplies that missing rule. No route is enrolled by this decision.

C avoids a review stop or an experimental compaction and can observe the request that full-local would ordinarily send. It still consumes an ordinary physical attempt and applicable task budget. Distinguish a veto evaluated by B, an override by full-local, and a veto actually enforced by a hold. At this one boundary C permits the same dispatch that an admit-if-either-fits rule would permit. Its separate decision, visible suspension and end of trial authority distinguish it from repeatedly choosing the smaller reservation. C measures B's predicted withholding; it does not impose or measure the downstream conversion cost of enforcing it. A can collect disposition and operational cost evidence despite withholding the candidate's own usage. Compaction has independent feasibility, dispatch and handoff checks, so continuation and a single successful compaction send would never have been guaranteed.

**Exact matched candidate.** For C, use the finalized full-local-feedback candidate as the subject of the matched comparison. Retain its canonical payload reference and hash, both reservations, identical allowance and applicable outcome reserve, route/configuration version, anchor basis and mode. Only call it an exact veto observation if full-local fits and the eligible anchor exceeds the allowance on those very bytes. Dispatch reads that immutable input. Do not insert a veto warning, recalculate feedback, or reassemble from newer task state and still label it the same request. Full-local admission remains subject to cancellation, budgets, instruction changes and existing recovery policy.

There is a distinct boundary if the enrolled assembler first detects a veto on a payload carrying *anchored* feedback. Its bytes can differ from the suspended-mode payload even with identical selected content. Preserve the earlier decision, evaluate both reservations on the finalized full-local version, and link both identities if they differ. A response on the latter can score only that version. If the disagreement disappears on it, an ordinary full-local send may still proceed under C; report the original veto as unmatched and the sent version as agreement. The fallback disposition and the exact-match validation result are separate. The offline fixture should exercise both preserved and disappearing disagreement, including the single-token boundary. No historical sample establishes how often either occurs. [Feedback assembly and byte-exact preview](../../src/pdfmd/working_context.py) and [freeze and dispatch](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py).

**Durability and interpretation.** Commit the suspension and enrollment disposition once against a stable decision ID, with immutable references sufficient to recover the selected candidate. A crash before freeze must resume that disposition. A crash after freeze must reuse or reconcile the existing record. Preserve a non-dispatch reason where applicable. Count a linked physical attempt only if one is actually started and retain ambiguity about remote execution after interruption. A prior accepted reply wins on reconciliation; existing retry and Continue gates still apply. Count this as a full-local override attempt, separate from a trial-budget attempt that only the anchor could admit. [Preparation and Continue](../../src/pdfmd/runtime.py)

For question 7, trace candidate hash and trial veto to suspension, disposition, actual request/attempt, and outcome. Separate accepted with attributable input, accepted without usable input, known failure, unknown and never dispatched. Compare normalized input with both reservations and the configured input allowance on the same payload. Acceptance alone does not measure upstream headroom or prove a general veto rule wrong. An unclassified failure does not validate it, and fees may remain unknown. First verify the identity and restart cases offline; no provider call or trial execution follows from this specification.

### Question 7 offline veto recovery and feedback identity (2026-11-04)

**Executed scope.** The approved analysis-only fixture uses new synthetic workspaces, production SQLite Store transactions, the full-local feedback assembler and counter, canonical encoding, immutable input storage and Nexus.freeze. The candidate, anchor inputs, trial records, slot protocol and alternative feedback labels are synthetic. The anchor prices the fixture's added messages with ordinary BPE on top of injected prior usage. No historical task or provider participated. This exercises persistence primitives and a candidate specification on disk; the application has no trial coordinator to certify.

| Interruption or boundary | Observed after reopening |
| --- | --- |
| Child process terminated between decision and suspension writes inside one transaction | Neither write survived. With the basis unchanged, fresh selection chose X again. An immutable candidate staged before the transaction was insufficient to establish a veto. |
| Both writes made, child terminated immediately before commit | Trial remained active and there was no decision. With a newer Y then committed, fresh preparation selected Y and fit. Recovering the identical old view would require an earlier durable basis binding. If such a dependent receipt exists, honor it. |
| Child terminated after commit, before freeze; Y arrived afterward | Trial was suspended. Recovery used the committed X and C disposition, froze the evaluated payload and retained the same request and hash on another reopen. The synthetic anchor was 7,176, full-local 1,254 and the allowance 7,168. There was one decision, one prepared request and zero attempts. |
| Another task already occupied the slot | In an ordered interleaving across independent Store handles, the old simulated attempt could settle. New claims were denied both before and after it released the slot. This checks one schedule, not simultaneous-process contention or real transport. |

**Boundary experiment.** On a single finalized full-local-feedback payload with synthetic anchor 7,169 and allowance 7,168, exact comparison yielded a veto and suspension. An illustrative two-token band on the identical bytes yielded full-local disposition without suspension. This tests the proposed classification path, not a justification for that width. No request was sent. Separately, with the same basis between each pair and internally consistent displayed values on synthetic anchored renders, changing feedback moved the anchored estimate from 7,169 to 7,165 in one example and from 7,163 to 7,169 in another. A third pair exceeded capacity on both versions (7,173 and 7,169) but still had different hashes. The earlier 34 sends never carried the proposed anchored feedback. They cannot bound a future renderer's digit, sign, padding, method-label or token-boundary effects. The self-inclusive reservation of the existing feedback loop applies to its own render and method.

**Reconciliation.** Finalize the full-local-feedback payload first and evaluate both reservations on its exact bytes for a C comparison. If C overrides a veto, freeze that same payload, retain the decision and check the ordinary task send budget on any eventual dispatch. A passing preliminary screen cannot authorize dispatch of a different anchored-feedback rendering. Recheck the actual finalized payload with the recorded basis. If the verdict changes, preserve both identities and distinguish a matched comparison from an unsent alternative. Prefer exact recounting; no indeterminate band is selected. A band would deliberately change trial authority even on a known exact payload. The tests above supply no evidence about real upstream acceptance, usage or fees.

**Reproduction and remaining coverage.** From the project root, run the existing project Python with `-B .local/veto-lifecycle-20261104-f6ec1244/check.py`. Each invocation creates a unique subdirectory. The complete result for this review is `.local/veto-lifecycle-20261104-f6ec1244/run-5iqm6ruk/results.json`. Three child processes exited abruptly by design; the parent verified each exit and the reopened records. Remaining question 7 work includes integrated lifecycle and retry fixtures if the feature is implemented, independently checked shadow/preview equivalence, structural edit coverage and product-level outcomes. An enrolled live interval still requires separate authorization, an explicit budget and prospectively declared checks. [Store](../../src/pdfmd/storage.py), [feedback assembly](../../src/pdfmd/working_context.py), [freeze and dispatch budget](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py).

### Question 7 decision order, shadow and retry (2026-11-04)

**Review status.** The two earlier fixture results agree on every shared field. Their three boundary pairs exercise the reservation of the anchor on two different feedback renderings. The full-local estimate fits the first payload in all three, so none exercises an anchor-only admission. A loop is a possible consequence of repeatedly rebuilding on a changed verdict, not an observed loop or a requirement of C. A fixed order makes the disposition reproducible. The following is a candidate refinement for the first trial.

1. Finalize full-local feedback (Pf) and compare both estimates on exactly Pf with the same route, allowance and reserved outcome. Within an enrolled trial with an eligible anchor, use Pf when both fit. If only full-local fits, apply the enrolled first-veto disposition (C default, A hold). If both block, return to ordinary full-local handling. This explicitly chooses ordinary feedback on agreement, even though two fitting methods can report different headroom. Ineligible, off, suspended and lockout rules remain separate.
2. Only if Pf fits under the anchor alone, consider a finalized anchored-feedback version (Pa). Reprice **both** methods on Pa, retaining the basis. Record preliminary agreement/disagreement, final agreement/disagreement and dispatch disposition separately. Exactly one or two finalized forms are considered per candidate; each form may require internal self-inclusive feedback iterations. New candidates after a deferral are separate opportunities. Failure to produce a stable, supported Pa needs an explicit fallback and reason. No numeric tolerance or return edge to rebuild Pf was used.
3. If only the anchor fits Pa, it is eligible for the trial slot and budget, subject to the usual gates. If both fit Pa, sending it would change the outcome relative to the ordinary Pf path, but would supply no evidence for the claim that only an anchor admitted those final bytes. Recommendation: if this send is allowed, keep it subject to the trial slot and exposure budget and count it separately as agreement on an alternate payload. This broadens which physical attempts consume that budget and remains an explicit choice. Alternatively discard this alternate and use ordinary Pf handling. If the anchor blocks Pa, discard it and take ordinary Pf handling with a recorded rendering loss. Distinguish both-block from full-only-fit on that speculative form. Under this single-pass proposal only a veto on Pf triggers first-veto suspension. There is no C override on Pa. These classifications precede any physical send and do not establish task benefit.

**Decision cases.** Exhaustive synthetic testing covered all 16 combinations of two fit flags on each of two forms. There was no second-form evaluation except after anchor-only fit on Pf. Six of the seven terminal classifications also have native serialized fixtures with the real full-local estimator and a synthetic increment rule. At allowance 7,168, Pf had full-local 7,169 and anchor 7,159 in one case; Pa had 7,163 and 7,154. In another case the final values were 7,178 and 7,169, so neither admitted Pa. An anchor block coupled with a full-local fit on Pa after an anchor-only Pf was covered by the scalar truth table only. The first fixture's three prior boundary pairs now terminate on Pf (two vetoes and one agreement). The results are structural tests, not measurements of these outcomes on either historical route.

**Shadow and recovery.** A fixture-only observer records its output outside the payload. Separately assembled shadow-on and shadow-off normal requests were compared byte for byte against the historical immutable inputs and against each other, including the complete governing capacity and deferral selections. Synthetic controls also cover a shadow veto and unavailable anchor evidence. For a synthetic frozen request carrying anchored feedback, two production Nexus send attempts through an in-memory mock transport, separated by store reopen and a newer synthetic usage pointer, delivered identical bytes to the mock; neither reached any provider. The production inspect preview refused an incompatible baseline. A fixture renderer supplied with the frozen X reproduced it; using newer Y failed the same byte gate. Preview capacity still uses the current estimator. This verifies the recovery mechanism and exposes the missing real anchored preview adapter; it does not validate integrated anchor pricing.

**Structural counts.** All 32 normal sends (25 A and 7 B) reproduced the frozen payload and the same shadow-on/off governing assessment, including six B requests with an existing deferral. The two compaction sends are outside normal-anchor scope. The 28 eligible normal pairs yielded 182 constructed, byte-changing variants. Notes (28), index pin (26), release argument (26), release result (26), release image (24) and compound release plus notes (26) all first failed the task/policy check. For 26 forced retained-group deferrals, the first failure was retained-history comparison. That deferral case imposes a substitution through the real group renderer; it does not establish that normal admission would choose it. Cases unavailable because an item did not yet exist were not generated. Zero qualifying edited variants measures this rule on these deliberately changed inputs, not field availability or upstream accuracy.

**Eligibility compatibility.** On A #2 to #3, changing just the estimate-kind label on either the predecessor or candidate changed the tested invariant-core result from eligible to layout rejection. A Pa send can therefore break eligibility on the following turn under the very rule previously scored on these runs. A versioned, recognized anchored-feedback form needs its own positive and negative eligibility tests before multi-turn trial results can be interpreted. The existing strict reader is appropriate for its original input format.

**Reproduction.** Analysis scripts and JSON results are in the ignored folder `.local/estimator-decision-order-20261104-dbd6334a/`. The decision-order run uses `order.py` and `order.json`; independent controls use `layout.py` and `shadow_control.py`. The retry run uses `retry.py` and `retry-run/result.json`. The structural matrix uses `matrix.py` with the run label and a fresh output path. Run A uses the saved run-matched assembler, Run B the current assembler; catalogs are opened immutable and query-only, and blob restaging can only verify existing identical content. The structural fixture imposes each hypothetical edit without executing the agent or applying policy. It tests the prior invariant-core eligibility rule; later conditional zero-credit release authority is a separate unimplemented rule. [Feedback and preview](../../src/pdfmd/working_context.py), [assembly and admission](../../src/pdfmd/context.py), [freeze and send](../../src/pdfmd/nexus.py).

### Question 2 revisited: unit matching and pin insertion (2026-11-04)

**Decision order.** The working first-trial table charges the slot and exposure budget whenever it permits a physical Pa send that ordinary Pf handling would have withheld. Count a send on which both estimators fit Pa separately from one on which only the anchor fits those exact bytes. If Pa is discarded because the anchor blocks it, record rendering loss and return to ordinary Pf handling. Even if full-local fits Pa, this speculative result is not a governing veto and cannot suspend the trial. The earlier full-only-on-Pa case was a scalar truth-table case, not one of the native fixtures. No extra top-level counter or tolerance is needed to distinguish its reason. Only the governing veto on Pf triggers suspension.

**What v1 excluded.** Charging the replacement task message covers local text for that control. A changed policy identity can also signal a different pin, an omitted tool body, moved pixels, a new receipt or a changed automatic deferral elsewhere in the request. Ignoring the policy hash without examining those effects would remove a guard. The 182 edited transitions all failed v1. This establishes the need for a distinct whole-unit release path if the proposed question 5 rule is to govern a request. It does not demonstrate that a stable changed policy disables anchoring on every later turn. Usage from the next accepted compatible full-local request can establish the next anchor. Run B had a deferred newly arriving result; its later reuse differs from deferring previously measured content.

**Offline candidate v2.** This two-step fixture starts with a reproduced unedited candidate whose predecessor, usage convention, route/settings, outer envelope, system, retained history and appended batch pass v1. The pre-edit flat anchor with the provisional 6% incremental text reserve must fit. It then checks one declared edit on the assembled candidate. Replace and charge both new control messages in full. Keep task/source/amendment identity fixed, permit only the edit's named control fields, and verify the resulting policy hash against the projected state. Recognize a versioned feedback shape and known method labels. An unknown field or label needs an explicit reader or fallback. A known label alone cannot authenticate the recorded basis. This fixture tests shape and specific field checks, not the entire proposed runtime schema.

Match unchanged ordered units by their actual representation and occurrence. The fixture associates calls and receipts with the saved argument/result references in batch order and checks image labels and native parts. A replacement must be attributable to the selected reference and the native assembler's edit. Removed units receive zero credit. Charge the gross replacement, including a separately inserted pin message. If the selected reference was already deferred, its own deferral-list entry can disappear; a change for another reference requires fallback in this experiment. An arbitrary ordered subsequence is insufficient: it can be empty after rewriting history, and equal content need not be the same occurrence. New system/tool definitions, new amendments, protocol or accounting changes, unexplained reorderings, compound policy updates and forced deferral of retained content do not acquire eligibility through this rule. Pins and notes were tested as experimental extensions; structural matching does not grant admission authority. The fixture compares known assembler projections and does not implement a production provenance or pricing engine.

**Pin experiment.** On the pinned offline Kimi JSON-tool template, compare the full candidate to a control that removes only the newly inserted pin message(s), leaving all other reassembled content identical. Independently compare the entire edit with the pre-edit candidate. Local pin text counts use the decoded o200k projection; template text positions exclude image processing. Even a pin of an existing reference can move native pixels, replace a tool result and alter feedback. Recognizing the same asset does not authorize reusing its cost across changed placements or views. No edited request has attributable provider usage.

**Results on the same 182 edits.** All original case keys, v1 first-failure reasons and governing no-edit shadow records reconciled. The narrow experimental matcher recognized 130 transitions (105 A, 25 B): 28 notes, 26 argument releases, 26 result releases, 24 image releases and 26 index pins. The 26 compound edits and 26 forced deferrals stayed on full-local by design. Fifteen edits changed the deferral-list membership of the selected reference only, including five unsupported compound cases. No case exhibited an unrelated reference changing deferral here. A strict whole-list equality gate would have rejected ten otherwise matched cases. Matched means structurally accounted for on the restricted assembler path; it establishes neither post-edit fit nor accurate reservations.

On 24 successive edited notes pairs, unchanged notes and policy qualified again under v1 when the preceding synthetic request was given usable fixture usage. At B #5 to #6, holding each of notes, index pin, argument release, result release or image release stable gave the same result in all five tests. This refutes permanent loss under v1. How often real tasks change policy again before a fresh eligible response is unknown. A separate manifest control on A #2 to #3 recognized the synthetic anchored label in the old slot, new slot or both only when accompanied by the expected version and payload hash. Missing or wrong manifest and unknown label all failed. This is a layout test and conveys no route trust or usage authority.

On the 26 index-pin projections, isolating the inserted message added 296–306 Kimi text positions. The decoded local charge was 304–313, exceeding the isolated increment by 7–8 in every case. Across the entire edit the Kimi change ranged from −19,058 to +317 because the pin can replace or reassemble retained content. On B #5 the isolated **full view of the same result** added 484,081 positions versus 306 for the index. A separate native-image pin on that snapshot inserted a pixel occurrence while total image count stayed constant; its 602-position change is text-only. These three views are not interchangeable. The previous 178 release previews also distinguish complete-edit coverage from coverage of reassembly after pure deletion. Even with the 6% candidate reserve the latter had six misses (four among 123 retained-reference cases). This assumption is still empirical for releases, not a demonstrated template or route guarantee.

**Next choice.** Specify a bounded release adapter for supported whole-unit edits. Keep notes and index pins observable separately in shadow while deciding whether to grant them admission authority. Full/native pins, pin deletion, compound changes and forced deferral require their own scope and evidence. The 130 matches justify separating eligibility by operation; they do not justify opening every content change to zero credit. A production gate would need independently verified origin/occurrence, complete repricing of every changed or inserted unit exactly once, a fresh fit check and compatible route accounting. This review did not implement it.

**Reproduction.** In the ignored directory `.local/estimator-unit-matching-20261104-9d0d281b/`, `check.py` rebuilds the 182 matrix entries with the run-matched assemblers, `matcher.py` implements the candidate gate, and `kimi.py` uses the existing pinned renderer on canonicalized request data. `stable.py` checks adjacent candidates with an identical changed policy and explicitly synthetic accepted usage. `pin_views.py` compares three views at B #5. The final per-run files are `final-A.json` and `final-B.json`; `summarize.py` checks correspondence with the previous matrix. Catalogs are immutable and query-only. No provider calls, original task actions or modifications occur. [Policy identity](../../src/pdfmd/context_outcomes.py), [pin hydration and result projection](../../src/pdfmd/context.py), [preceding matrix](#question-7-decision-order-shadow-and-retry-2026-11-04).

### Question 2 and 3 pricing notes and index pins (2026-11-04)

**What was compared.** Reconstructed all 28 notes and 26 index-pin candidates from their frozen predecessors and reproduced the earlier unit matches. The 28 notes cases repeat the same 39-character English edit. None is an observed agent decision to change notes. The earlier reference-level deferral count also reconciles: five result releases and five index pins changed only the selected reference's membership among the matched cases, plus five unsupported compounds. These workloads cannot establish that notes are the most frequent operation.

For each edit, start with the predecessor's recorded input. Add one decoded BPE count over the new task control, inserted/changed retained messages, the ordinary appended batch and final feedback in actual message order. Assert that appended content and native image occurrences match the frozen unedited candidate; charge each selected occurrence once. Removed text receives zero credit. Add the recorded 4,096 allowance per newly appended image and separately evaluate zero or ceil(6% of new text). For pins the ablation omits only the changed retained tool receipt. Both arms still count the new pin and controls. Recompute fit against the unchanged input allowance of 122,880. All 54 full-pricing candidates fit at both rates. In the 6% arm the per-request reservations range from 5,241 to 85,313 for notes and 17,642 to 86,022 for index pins.

The absolute reservation uses historical route input as the prior. It has no matching usage on an edited response. For the **edit comparison**, subtract the unedited candidate's reservation at the same rate and compare that difference with edited minus unedited Kimi rendered text. For the **turn comparison**, compare the entire local new-text charge with edited Kimi text minus the predecessor's Kimi text. Do not compare a mixed-route absolute reservation directly with a Kimi template total or count the image allowance as covering text. The two comparisons ask different questions.

| Edit and charged units | Paired edit covered at 0% / 6% | Full Kimi text increment covered at 0% / 6% |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Notes, both controls and ordinary append | 24/28 / 24/28 | 26/28 / 28/28 |
| Index pin, controls and append, inserted pin only | 23/26 / 26/26 | 25/26 / 26/26 |
| Index pin, same charges plus replacement history receipt | 26/26 / 26/26 | 26/26 / 26/26 |

**Notes.** Removing only the task and feedback controls in a Kimi render isolates their gross cost. The local charge for the two new controls was 386–462 tokens and exceeded that gross cost on every short-note case by 12–15. No additional retained history unit changed. The complete edited render grew by three positions in each case. The local paired change fell short by one to three on four rows (A #2, #8, #18, #19) at both rates. Applying a percentage to both sides does not ensure their difference grows. A small marginal miss does not establish an unsafe absolute reservation. Conversely, a gross overcount on these short controls cannot establish transfer to arbitrary note text. Two zero-rate turn misses (A #2 and #13) also include the ordinary newly appended batch.

Four additional notes controls at B #5 used empty, repeated English, Han-rich and JSON-like text. The unchanged/empty control and the two non-Han stress edits had nonnegative gross and paired margins. The 20,400-character note made by repeating one 17-character Traditional Chinese phrase 1,200 times left the local control charge 4,787 below the gross Kimi control effect. The net edit shortfall was 4,795 at 0% and 3,571 at 6%; the entire new-text increment still fell short by 2,995 at 6%. It was structurally matched, fit the configured local allowance, and was never sent. This constructed counterexample isolates text transfer and cannot estimate frequency, establish a universal rate, or measure the historical route.

**Index pins.** On B #5 the pin message counted alone was 313, while the complete edited render grew by 317. Including control changes and joint counting made the pin-only reservation rise by exactly 317 at 0%. Including the changed retained receipt raised it by 731, a margin of 414. The zero-rate pin-only arm did miss on three other B rows by two to three tokens. With the receipt charged, all 26 paired margins were positive (minimum 380). These are gross reservations that give no credit for the old body. They are not estimates of the isolated price of the receipt. The earlier B #5 full-view example already exceeds the route input allowance in local insertion text alone. The native-image example compares template text positions, not vision slots or upstream input, and unchanged image count can hide a relocation. Neither example extends the index-pin validation to all pin types.

**Recommendation for the working specification.** A verified notes-only replacement needs no extra history charge or operation-specific penalty. Treat it as structurally eligible when the actual diff proves controls-only, and qualify governing trial authority by the same validated new-text accounting as other increments. The proposed no-reserve coverage condition did not hold for every comparison, and arbitrary notes have a demonstrated transfer risk. Keep unvalidated compositions on the applicable fallback/shadow path without restricting what notes the agent may write. The narrow index-pin pricing condition is satisfied on these fixtures when both the inserted view and every changed retained unit are priced exactly once. That version can advance as a candidate for a bounded named trial on supported views; pin-only pricing, full/native views and general promotion have no corresponding validation here. No rule, rate, or trial was activated.

**Evidence.** The ignored directory `.local/estimator-edit-pricing-20261104-4c667efd/` contains per-request `A.json` and `B.json`, separate gross-control and stress results in `notes-A.json` and `notes-B.json`, and `summary.json`. Run `price.py` or `notes.py` with the project Python, `-B`, run letter and a fresh output path. `summarize.py` verifies and combines the designated outputs. Baseline payload and render hashes were verified against prior evidence; original catalogs were immutable and query-only. The app, historical tasks and configuration were untouched. [Unit-matching basis](#question-2-revisited-unit-matching-and-pin-insertion-2026-11-04).

### Question 3 script share and Traditional Chinese tool text (2026-11-05)

**Review correction.** The long notes probe repeats one 17-character Traditional Chinese phrase 1,200 times. Ordinary counts for one instance were 17 o200k and 21 Kimi, and the repeated counts scaled exactly. Its word-piece patterns differ: o200k groups the enumeration comma with following text and Kimi separates it. The earlier short Traditional and Simplified examples were different strings. None estimates a document population. In an aligned synthetic translation both variants had the same 88.24% Han share, but their Kimi differences were +23.53% and −15.38%. Adding compressible spaces to a synthetic JSON field reduced the whole object's share to 8.2% while leaving an 18.29% undercount relative to the o200k count. No script-share threshold or 6% coverage guarantee follows from the stress test. The structural treatment of notes as replacement controls and index pins as charged insertions plus changed retained receipts stands. Text transfer applies to all newly charged units.

**Direct PDF evidence.** The user-supplied nine-page Six Policy Options PDF (5,915,465 bytes, SHA-256 aa59f53fe2486eceb4134a224c910a2692e7e216ba2f7e97a34afeef5921cde5) has extractable Traditional Chinese on every page, mixed with English and numerals. PyMuPDF found tables on pages 2 and 9. Extract sorted text in page order without stripping layout spaces. Accumulate non-overlapping complete pages until o200k reaches 4,000 ordinary tokens, retaining the final short block. The measured representation matters.

| Six Policy Options | o200k body | Kimi body | Body margin with 6% | Added local decoded text in tool fixture | Kimi template increment | Marginal reserve minus increment at 6% |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Pages 1–6 | 4,042 | 4,332 | −47 | 4,339 | 4,625 | −26 |
| Pages 7–9 | 2,379 | 2,507 | +15 | 2,534 | 2,661 | +25 |

The tool fixture varies a single synthetic result-text field in the same saved B #2 Chat Completions layout, holding the remaining messages, feedback and JSON-tool renderer fixed. These are changes between local renders, not accepted requests or complete frozen reservations. At individual-page granularity the 6% body reserve missed seven of nine pages, worst by 18. At a 50% Han/Kana/Hangul threshold all nine pages would pass the share screen, including all seven misses. At 30%, even the two pages still permitted would both miss; at 10% all nine would fall back. This is a measured tradeoff for this document and representation, not a population frequency or proof of overflow.

**Same book, different representations.** The 173-page original JRE scan produced zero native text on all pages. The supplied page-marked reference Markdown is a distinct representation. Its 15 blocks of at least 4,000 o200k tokens ranged from −2.11% to +5.79% transfer, all covered at 6%. The final 1,187-token block missed by one; ten of 173 individual page bodies missed, worst by 17. In a separate offline extraction, the installed Windows zh-Hant-HK OCR engine read physical pages 1–22, 76–97 and 152–173. These ranges include headings, numerals and tabular content. Uncorrected OCR inserts spaces between many Han characters. Across ten contiguous OCR blocks (seven long), ordinary counts totaled 36,580 o200k versus 46,930 Kimi. All ten missed at 6%; individual differences were +20.44% to +33.75%. As a sensitivity only, removing spaces strictly between two Han characters changed the totals to 25,675 versus 26,736; two of those same blocks still missed. This controlled variant is not a recommendation to alter source text or a fidelity assessment of the OCR. A separate 290-page news-writing PDF with predominantly Simplified text had negative transfer on all 45 long blocks. The books differ in content as well as orthography.

**Historical route evidence.** Of the 34 actual sends, 28 were eligible adjacent pairs and only 16 had unchanged images. On those 16 the appended batch exceeded the observed input increase by 22–65 local tokens. The usage-increase/batch ratio ranged from 0.838 to 0.997. Normalized newly charged blocks across all 28 had zero Han/Kana/Hangul characters. Tool result strings comprised 77.5% of the decoded appended batch characters in aggregate, including envelopes and other tool data. Changes in the task and feedback remain in the observed difference; added images, releases, accounting changes or a missing response can further confound it. Record signed residual, absolute new-text exposure, role, image/control changes, accounting version and attribution alongside any ratios. A net usage difference requires compatible usage on both ends. A route's declared field semantics alone do not calibrate its text proxy.

**Question 3 recommendation.** Keep separate script shares, absolute text volume and representation (native text, OCR, wrapped tool result) as validation and shadow diagnostics. No automatic script cutoff or runtime language classifier is selected. Falling back to full-local can withhold context and still uses a local text proxy. Prefer testing a reusable optional counter for an explicitly identified encoding on the charged text. In a narrow ablation the already-pinned Kimi vocabulary, counting the same decoded tool-text projection, matched the Kimi template increment exactly on both policy blocks and all ten raw OCR blocks before reserve. The two sides deliberately shared the encoding and changed only one field. This isolates vocabulary transfer; it validates neither full prompts, templates, visual accounting, routing aliases nor unknown models. For unknown encodings retain an explicit provisional profile; scope any expansion of trial authority to named route/profile evidence and explicit enrollment. Do not require a custom tokenizer for every new model, silently retune rates from observed usage, or extrapolate these textbook samples to the user's normal workload. The open choice is whether to specify this optional increment counter before considering any automatic composition gate.

**Offline record.** Counts, source hashes, extraction choices, threshold tables and the synthetic tool comparison are in `.local/script-share-review-20261105-326e69da/`. See `policy.json`, `pages-policy.json`, `jre-md.json`, `tool-and-ocr.json`, `matched-vocab.json`, `controls.json` and `route.json`. OCR scratch and page text stay private under that ignored folder. No provider or counting API calls, enrollment, task continuation or application/configuration changes occurred. ### Question 3 unknown encoding, weighted reserve and OCR spacing (2026-11-05)

**Mechanism.** On the ten saved JRE OCR blocks, cross the pinned ordinary BPE vocabularies (mergeable ranks) with both pre-tokenization patterns on identical text. The raw block counts and hashes reproduce the previous study. The columns below change only the split rule at fixed vocabulary. Counts are summed across independent block encodes.

| Ordinary vocabulary | o200k split | Kimi split |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| o200k | 36,580 | 44,681 |
| Kimi | 46,930 | 46,930 |

Of 15,863 literal ASCII spaces in runs bounded on both sides by Han characters, all 15,863 occupied their own single-space token under the native Kimi encoding. Under native o200k only 2,158 did. The o200k ranks with the Kimi pattern also separated all 15,863; Kimi ranks retained all 15,863 single-space tokens even with the o200k pattern. Counted by actual decoded token byte offsets. The pre-tokenization boundaries restrict possible merges; the available ranks determine which permitted merges occur. The swap added 8,101 tokens with o200k ranks and zero with Kimi ranks. This interaction prevents assigning one encoding-independent percentage of the difference to either mechanism. Removing only the bounded spaces gave four counts of 25,675, 26,288, 26,736 and 26,736 in the same row-major order. Raw Kimi was 28.3% higher than native o200k in aggregate. These tests identify an OCR-representation effect within this corpus; they do not rank extraction format against language in general or judge whether any source space should be removed in delivery.

**Proposed unknown-route rate.** Implemented the reviewer's arithmetic offline: let T be the decoded local text count, H the Han character count, and N the total number of Unicode characters of the newly charged text, including spaces, punctuation, controls and JSON structure. Add ceil(T × (0.06 + 0.28 × H/N)). Image reservations remain separate. This interpolates a 6% non-Han rate and a 34% Han rate. The observed maximum of 33.75% was a **whole-block** difference on mixed, spaced text. It was never a per-Han parameter. On these OCR bodies H/N was 17.6%–45.4%; the weighted rate therefore supplied only 10.9%–18.7%. The more inter-character spaces present, the larger the denominator can become.

| Comparison with pinned Kimi | 6% misses | Weighted misses | Effect of weighting |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Six Policy Options, two body blocks | 1/2 | 0/2 | Minimum surplus 267 tokens |
| JRE reference Markdown, 16 body blocks including the short tail | 1/16 | 0/16 | Total reserve rose from 3,874 to 13,479 |
| Raw JRE OCR, ten body blocks | 10/10 | 10/10 | Reserve rose from 2,200 to 5,814; worst shortfall 706 |
| Twelve synthetic tool-result comparisons on a frozen B layout | 11/12 | 10/12 | Both policy inserts covered (+330, +233); all OCR inserts short by 148–779 |

For the tool comparison, calculate each method on the entire charged projection before and after filling one synthetic result field, then compare the difference in the two reservations with the template increment. The illustration of 1,350 extra tokens on a 4,000-token addition approximates a flat 34% (+1,360); the weighted formula at the observed OCR shares adds about 437–748. On the actual 28 eligible saved pairs the new projections contained no Han, so this weighting gives identical decisions to 6%. The twelve edited candidates also fit the configured local allowance far from its boundary; none was sent. These facts establish neither a route limit nor a downstream conversion benefit.

**Other offline sensitivities.** Flat 34% covered the ten raw bodies, with a minimum surplus of 12 and total reserve of 12,442. Six percent plus one token per bounded Han space covered them with a minimum surplus of 297 but cost 18,063; it still missed one policy page and ten reference-Markdown pages. A text-only sensitivity that takes the higher of the two split-rule counts with o200k ranks and adds 6% covered these OCR bodies by as little as one token. All three use the target that motivated their examination. They are diagnostics, not independent validation of an unknown-encoding bound. Hybrid encodings do not establish an upstream tokenizer. The documents and the non-overlapping blocks within each remain correlated.

**Recommendation and open choice.** For a route whose ordinary encoding is explicitly known and versioned, specify an optional adapter for the entire encoding (pattern, ranks, special-token policy and chunking) on new text. It can reuse assets across matching routes. Whole-request admission still needs the appropriate protocol/rendering, image and uncertainty treatment. The current proxy alias has no established upstream encoding. Similar English deltas provide no such identification; even a matching text encoding would not identify template or image cost. For an unknown encoding, keep the generic full-local path and the previously scoped, explicitly enrolled trial boundary. Keep 6% labeled provisional and do not promote the proposed 6%–34% mix, flat 34%, or a spacing surcharge to governing authority on this evidence. This supplies a decision policy without claiming a universal safe numerical reserve. Keep absolute text exposure, script shares, inter-Han spacing, route/accounting version, method and note/tool/image/control changes in shadow comparisons. Record normalized usage differences and both reservations only where comparable accepted predecessor and successor usage exist. Preserve withheld and missing-usage cases separately.

A separately authorized conversion with representative Chinese inputs and attributable same-image normal pairs could discriminate whether this route exhibits a practically significant residual on those inputs. It cannot determine the tokenizer's identity, establish typical rates for other models, or validate routine admission from one run. Before selecting a new default, predeclare the candidate, independent source/OCR/model or route holdout and boundary cost measures. No provider or counting call, conversion, settings change or implementation was authorized or performed here.

**Reproducible evidence.** Use the project Python with `-B` to run `check.py` with the named policy PDF followed by `summarize.py` in `.local/unknown-encoding-review-20261105-055f9ddb/`. Scripts write new scalar JSON artifacts only and expect the already pinned local assets and immutable prior inputs; copy the scripts to a new ignored sibling folder for a rerun. `results.json` records per-block counts, byte-level separator totals, rates, paired charges and headroom. `summary.json` groups the results. [Original extraction and renderer basis](#question-3-script-share-and-traditional-chinese-tool-text-2026-11-05).

### Question 3 cross-split trial candidate (2026-11-05)

**Working first-specification recommendation.** Prefer option (a) over plain 6% for an explicitly enrolled, bounded trial on a route with unknown ordinary encoding. On the same decoded newly charged text, count with the shared o200k mergeable ranks twice, once under the o200k split rule and once under the pinned Kimi split rule. Take the higher **total** and add ceil(6% of that total) once. The native fixture used the existing 8,192-character chunk treatment for each count. Images and output reserve remain separately identified. This affects candidate admissions in both directions within the agreed trial rules. Off, suspended, unsupported and full-local fallback paths retain their governing semantics. It does not require knowing the target encoding or loading its vocabulary to estimate. It does depend on the two specific patterns, shared ranks, projection and chunking. Record their versions/digests, both counts, the applied rate and actual headroom. Runtime support would require packaging the second pattern and a second counting pass; the private study asset is not a deployed dependency. Known encodings can use a declared optional adapter. Weighted Han rates and per-space surcharges remain offline comparisons. No trial, release, setting change or implementation is selected by this recommendation.

**What reproduced.** On the earlier ten raw OCR bodies this candidate reserved 10,786 tokens above the o200k point total and covered all ten by at least one token. It was the least expensive of the compared candidates that covered all ten on that metric. On the 46 news-writing blocks, base 6% already covered every body (minimum surplus 214). The total reserve rose from 12,084 to 20,281 under cross-split, 49,779 under Han weighting and 68,381 under flat 34%. No added coverage was observed on that group. Direct inspection found zero ordinary Kimi rank entries beginning with ASCII space followed by a complete Han character, versus 1,324 in the pinned o200k ranks. That statement concerns these specific assets and byte pattern; it does not establish a general rule for other scripts, vocabularies or deployments.

**Prospective page check.** Fixed both candidate formulas, the two previously unused contiguous ranges (23–75 and 98–151), and the 4,000-o200k-token block rule before scoring. Rendered all 107 remaining JRE pages with the earlier settings and the same local Traditional Chinese OCR engine. Kept short tails. This adds 16 non-overlapping blocks held out by page, sharing the book, engine and Kimi target with the original ten. Each synthetic tool result uses a frozen B layout and a single filled versus empty result field. The reported *field margin* compares the increase in local reservation with the increase in rendered Kimi text. The *turn margin* separately compares the full new-text charge with the edited render minus its predecessor render, including control recharges. Neither is a provider usage observation.

| OCR population and metric | Plain 6% | Cross-split + 6% | Flat 34% |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Original ten tool fields, misses (worst margin) | 10 (−1,240) | 0 (+11) | 0 (+44) |
| New sixteen tool fields, misses (worst margin) | 16 (−1,275) | 0 (+10) | 1 (−8) |
| New sixteen full text increments, misses (worst margin) | 15 (−848) | 0 (+437) | 0 (+555) |
| New sixteen raw bodies, total reserve above ordinary o200k | 3,842 | 19,438 | 21,733 |

Cross-split cost 436–1,310 additional tokens over plain 6% on the new complete charged projections. On a future input with identical other costs this difference can reverse a local fit if that is all the headroom left. All sixteen synthetic inputs still fit the saved allowance with at least 111,853 tokens left under this variant. On the 28 eligible historical pairs the two charging rules agreed exactly, with zero changed fits. The 1,256-token shortfall quoted in the review was for an original raw body; it was not an admission failure. Unknown-route under-reporting could also allow an upstream success with biased reported usage, so terminal errors alone cannot validate the reserve.

**Residual errors and question 7.** On the two policy tool inserts the cross-split field margins were +54 and +76. The earlier small page-level misses do not establish a fixed framing cost. As a length control, repeating the same extracted policy page (page 2) 1, 10 and 100 times produced tool-field shortfalls of 6, 51 and 505. At 100 repetitions, the entire new-text charge also missed by 78. The 1,200-repeat unspaced Traditional Chinese phrase still missed its field increment by 3,576. Repetition demonstrates scaling and supplies no estimate of natural frequency. This is a counter for one transfer mechanism, not a numerical upper bound for unknown models. Keep plain 6% alongside the trial method for offline/shadow comparison, without changing frozen feedback in off mode. Question 7 should compare extra headroom and changed decisions on the same finalized bytes, distinguish raw/field/turn errors, and score real attributable input against the actual reservation and allowance when available. Preserve the opportunity, method, gate, disposition, attempt and outcome distinctions already agreed. The decisive outstanding evidence is a separately authorized route-level observation and independent source/encoding coverage; neither an avoided compaction nor a general safety claim follows from these local counts.

**Reproduction.** The ignored folder `.local/cross-split-review-20261105-816451f6/` contains the pre-scoring design, OCR selection and private page scratch, `check.py`, `summarize.py`, `results.json` and `summary.json`. The project interpreter and the earlier pinned renderer and local OCR executable were used. No new assets were downloaded.
